A call now waits `CALL_TIMEOUT` (120 s, the bound the agent already gives an act over its own link) and then raises **naming the method**, so the next one says which RPC never answered; the calls that mean to block pass their own — `wait` its timeout plus slack, `send --wait` the same — which is the shape `HostAgent._route_act` gives those two between hosts, applied one layer down where the reply is read. And **a call that timed out is not a connection that dropped** (review of PR #315): `AgentStuck` is a subclass of `AgentUnavailable`, so every handler is unchanged, and the one caller that reconnects re-raises it rather than remaking — a wedged agent hidden behind a *nothing changed* at the deadline would be worse than the hang. **It is not only a test fix**: a live worker sitting in `ao send` never came back while its lead read it as `working`.

**Watch (added 2026-09-27; check by 2026-10-04, main's CI runs: `gh run list --branch main`):** `tests/test_mail.py::test_the_debt_has_a_bound_of_its_own_and_is_never_pruned` failed once on the 3.12 runner (run 36325996046, main at `744c4d99`, a ledger-only commit, `KeyError: 'm-738fe5195113'`). It passed on 3.13, on the rerun, and five times locally. A second failure makes it this entry's fifth flake, named here with its run.

**Watch (added 2026-10-03; check by 2026-10-10, the same list):** `tests/test_containers.py::test_the_home_derives_a_container_nodes_reach_when_it_dials_in` failed once on the 3.13 runner (run 37170470079, PR #947 at `4b0a3e8a`, a docs-only commit): its `wait_for(... "cm" not in agent.supervision and node.config.is_file(), timeout=5.0)` at `tests/test_containers.py:621` ran out. It passed on 3.12 in the same run and on the rerun of the failed job. A second failure makes it a flake of this entry, named here with its run.

**Location:** `tests/test_cli.py::test_send_wait`, `tests/test_agent.py::test_the_tick_retires_a_branch_claim_the_session_abandoned`, `tests/conftest.py` (`derived`), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`_reconcile`, `rpc_kill`, `_killed_at`)

**Why:** (1) PR #180's first CI run (2026-09-17 ~05:29Z, run 35185832437, test (3.12)): `ao send sw --wait --timeout 5 go` returned `prompt-stalled: … showed no activity within 4.69821 s` against a `hookstub` session — the send path was untouched by that PR, and the job passed on re-run. (2) PR #189's CI run (2026-09-17 ~06:37Z, test (3.12)): the tick had not yet retired a derived `TD-077` `claimed` entry when the test asserted `progress == []`; PR #189 renamed a grant and did not touch `sessionorc.reports` or the tick. Passed on re-run and three times locally. Both are timing-shaped (a bound a slow runner can miss), the kind TD-033 fixed for two other tests by waiting on the real signal instead of a duration.

**Fix:** ✅ both, 2026-09-17 (PR #199), each by waiting on the real signal rather than on a duration or on a task's identity.

(1) `test_send_wait` posted one `working`/`idle` pair on a 0.3 s and a 0.6 s sleep. `rpc_send` captures the revision it waits for **after** `_submit` — a paste, a settle and a composer check — so on a slow runner both transitions can land before that read: the record is idle, its revision is already past, nothing else ever moves it, and the stall window expires. That is `showed no activity within 4.69821 s` exactly. The thread now produces turns *until the send returns*, so whichever cycle lands after the read satisfies both waits at any load, and it ends on idle so the settled state the command prints is unchanged.

(2) `test_the_tick_retires_a_branch_claim_the_session_abandoned` — and five other assertions of the same shape, which had the same latent race — did `tick()` then `await agent._derive_task`. But `tick()` starts a derivation only when `_derive_task` is None or done, and the `agent` fixture runs a live tick loop at `FAST_TICK`: a background tick can already own an in-flight derive whose snapshot predates the `git checkout` the test just made, so the test's own `tick()` starts nothing and the await returns the *stale* answer. The new `conftest.derived(agent, check)` drains an in-flight derive, clears the two caches, ticks, awaits the derive it started, and re-checks — bounded, and a stale derive costs a retry instead of a failure.

**Done when** each has either a deterministic wait or failure diagnostics, and neither fails in ten consecutive CI runs. **Still open:** the PR #192 hang, which no longer has a way to recur unnoticed (the 15-minute cap ends it and `-vv` names it), so the next occurrence closes this entry one way or the other.

**Related:** TD-033 (archived: the same class, two other tests), TD-025 (archived: `test_cli.py` idle-wait diagnostics).

## TD-064: Claude Code's peer messages reach a session around the mail gates, and block an unattended one

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-17 (anchor session, watching the samscrape team)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** live-check #431
**Status:** **Read 2026-10-05 (grinder-ao-1), waits for a live event: owner anchor.** Not pressable on a scratch home: `scripts/look_home.py` gives its sessions a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` of its own, so no logged-in Claude Code runs there to send or receive a peer message, and the live copy is never pressed. Read live, from `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` and each process's `AGENTORC_SESSION`: every unattended session runs on a `+unattended` layer (`grind+unattended.json` for both grinders, `grind-fable+unattended.json` for the techlead and the designer), and the two interactive entry sessions on `grind-fable.json` without it. Left: the refusal itself, read the next time a session sends a worker a peer message (TD-297's live events list it: *a SendMessage to a worker refused*). **Re-owned 2026-10-04 (Paul): the live check is a grinder's, a press on a scratch home (`scripts/look_home.py`), never the live copy.** The press: a peer-channel message from a second Claude session to an unattended one is refused and its sender told. Seen live: `crossSessionInbound: refuse` in every unattended settings layer, the interactive ones without it. Built — live check pending. **All three parts built 2026-09-22 (PR #431, `grinder-ao-1`):** (a) `hooks_settings(…, unattended=True)` adds `crossSessionInbound: refuse`, written to its own `<profile>[+cadence]+unattended.json` and passed by `launch` for an unattended session only (`tests/test_claude_adapter.py::test_an_unattended_layer_refuses_the_tools_peer_messages`); (b) the `held-peer-message` rule in `screen_rules.toml` — keyed on the two menu lines, each anchored to its whole line, **not** on the heading, because the heading sits more than `TAIL_LINES` (15) above the menu once a preview is drawn and the tick would never see it; the fixture is the real panel from `manager-sam-1`'s run log of 2026-09-22, body neutralised; like every screen rule it applies only once no hook is fresher than `STALL_AFTER`, so a panel drawn soon after a hook reads `needs-you` up to twenty minutes late (`tests/test_screen.py::test_a_held_peer_message_needs_a_person_and_prose_about_it_does_not`); (c) the sentence in `ao --skill` (Mail) and in each built-in brief. **Left: the live check** — after the next promote, an unattended session that another session messages through the peer channel shows no panel and the sender is told it was refused (the value `refuse` is from the design, not yet seen working); the board carries it. Before: **decided and designed 2026-09-22 (Paul: *refuse them*; the anchor, §4.10 *The tool's own peer channel*).** Seen again 2026-09-22 at 13:39 MDT: Paul's interactive samscrape session sent `manager-sam-1` a claim notice, the panel sat on the manager's screen, `ao explain` read it as `idle (hook)` — *no screen rule matched* — and the anchor pressed Deliver. TD-102, written that afternoon for the classifier and the briefs' silence, is folded into this entry (archived). The setting's values are `accept` / `hold` (the default) / `refuse`, and it rides the `--settings` layer. Pickable, three parts in one PR or two: **(a)** `hooks_settings` (`src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/__init__.py`) adds `crossSessionInbound: refuse` for an unattended launch and nothing for an interactive one — the layer is chosen per profile and cadence today, so the unattended variant is a third file or a key `write_hooks_file` takes from the create; **(b)** a `screen_rules.toml` rule beside `trust-dialog`: `any` the panel's heading *Held message from another session*, `all` its two options (*Deliver this message to Claude* / *Deny*), `needs-you`, `pending: {kind: question, text: "a message from another Claude Code session is held (deliver or deny in the terminal)"}`; **(c)** one sentence in `src/agentorc/skill.md` under the report channels and in each `src/agentorc/briefs/*.md`'s rules. The two facets below stand: (a) is why a delivered message still waits for the round's `ao wait`, and (b) is TD-088's shape. Earlier: seen twice on 2026-09-17, an hour apart, from the same sender to the same lead; answered by hand both times. It recurs on every such message. Nothing built; the policy is Paul's to choose. **Two more facets, seen on the fourth and fifth messages (2026-09-17 ~23:00 MDT):** (a) once the dialog is answered the message is only *queued* — a lead blocked in its round's `ao wait` reads it when that call returns, up to nine minutes later, where `ao msg` wakes the same lead within a second; the sender, seeing no reaction, resent, and the resend was held in turn (*my previous confirmation expired unapproved*). (b) the card keeps reading `needs-you` after the dialog is gone, for minutes, because nothing tells the host agent the question was answered until the session's next hook event — and a lead inside one long Bash call fires none. **Next:** built; what is left is the live check.

**Location:** `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/` (the settings layer a launch writes), design §4.10 (the mediation property), §4.2 (what `needs-you` means for an unattended session), ADR `docs/decisions/2026-09-16-agent-messaging-prior-art.md`

**Why:** Claude Code has its own session-to-session messaging (a per-session socket under `/run/user/<uid>/cc-socks/`, peers discovered through `~/.claude/sessions/`). On 2026-09-17 Paul's interactive samscrape session sent `orchestrator-sam-1` his 22 board decisions that way. Claude Code **held** the message — *the sending session's permission mode class doesn't match this session's … set "crossSessionInbound" to "accept"* — and put a two-option dialog in the lead's pane. The host agent reported `needs-you` with *A message from another session needs your approval*; the lead, unattended and blocked in `ao wait`, could not answer it, and the team had no lead until the anchor session chose *Deliver* with `ao keys`. Two separate problems: **(1) it is a second channel.** §4.10 builds mail so that every message between sessions passes one gate, is tallied, bounded and visible on the card; a peer message passes none of that, and had the modes matched it would have been delivered with nobody seeing it. **(2) it stops an unattended session.** No policy answers that dialog, and no brief can: the session is not running a turn while it is up.

**Fix:** decide the policy, then write it into the settings layer every agentorc launch already carries (design §4.2): `crossSessionInbound` set so an unattended session **never blocks** — *deny* keeps mail the only channel and costs a person the shortcut Paul used; *accept* keeps the shortcut and leaves a channel the gates do not see. Whichever is chosen, the classifier should recognise the held-message dialog by name rather than as a generic question, and the design should say in §4.10 that the tool's own messaging exists and what agentorc does about it. A person who wants to tell a lead something has `ao msg` and the card's **Message**.

**Done when** an unattended session started by agentorc cannot be stopped by another Claude Code session's message, and §4.10 names the channel and the policy.

**Related:** TD-052 (mail), design §9 invariant 13, ADR 2026-09-16 (why agentorc builds its own messaging rather than using Claude Code's — this is the other half of that decision: what to do about theirs).

## TD-071: Org page review, 2026-09-18 — what was built, and the ideas that were not

**Priority:** Low
**Added:** 2026-09-18 (the anchor session; Paul's review of the Org page)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** build
**Status:** **Paul, 2026-10-07:** snoozed to 2026-10-14. Open — **items 1 and 2 built 2026-09-23 (PR #499, grinder-ao-1); item 8 is Paul's canvas.** Was: **items 1 and 2 designed 2026-09-23 (the anchor): §4.5a rows *team card: Forget all* and *team header ✉ n*; build them as the rows say, one page PR each or together (`src/agentorc/ui/`: `group_head.html`, `org.html`, `app.js`); item 8 is Paul's canvas.** Forget all is n Forgets behind one confirm, skipping and naming every card with the dirty / unpushed flag (not refusing the team on one flag — the per-card Forget refuses nothing but a suspended record, `rpc_remove`); the unread sum on a folded header is display only, the mail staying under §4.10. Item 2's open question is answered by the code: a start under the same name moves the old record's mail to the new session (`_supersede`, TD-081), so an unread note outlives the run and lands in the next one's inbox — by design, and now visible on the fold. Was: **items 1 and 2 approved by Paul 2026-09-23: the anchor writes their two §4.5a rows in one design PR, then a grinder builds; item 8 stays Paul's canvas.** Was: a list to pick from, none approved; **items 3, 4 and 5 are settled by TD-095's card (2026-09-21)**, **6 and 7 by the mockups catching up (2026-09-22, grinder-ao-2)**, below. Left: 1 and 2 (each a §4.5a row first — the anchor's design), 8 (Paul's canvas). Built in the same PR as this entry: a team with nothing live keeps its card (Start on it, its dead cards folded, below the live teams and *No team*), the **Teams** strip retired, state glyphs on every pill, *Person inbox* → **Inbox**. **Next:** what is left is item 8, Paul's canvas.
**Location:** `src/agentorc/ui/` (templates, `app.js`, `app.css`)

**Why:** on 2026-09-18 the page was seven cards, six of them dead, under a strip of three stopped teams: nothing on it said which dead card was whose, and what was running was one card among them. The review fixed the grouping; these are what it turned up beside it, kept here so they are not lost with the conversation.

**Ideas, cheapest first:**
1. ~~**Forget all** on a stopped team's card~~ — **built 2026-09-23 (PR #499)**, as design §4.5a *team card: Forget all* says: — seven exited cards is seven Forget clicks today. A team-level control: §4.5a row first. It should refuse while any of the team's sessions is not ready to close (unpushed work, as `orchestrator-ao-1`'s *305 unpushed* flag showed on 2026-09-18).
2. ~~**Unread mail on a folded team**~~ — **built 2026-09-23 (PR #499)**, as design §4.5a *team header ✉ n* says: — the header of a folded team says nothing about its sessions' unread counts (`tdgrind-1` held 19 on 2026-09-18). A `✉ n` beside the fold count. Unread mail on an exited session is by design as far as it goes: an unread entry never ages out (§4.10), and a *resumed* conversation takes the old record's mail with it (`agent.py`, `_supersede` → `_move_mail`). **Not verified:** what a fresh `ao team start` under the same name does with the dead record's inbox. Most of those 19 were claim notes, stale within the hour — worth deciding whether an unread `note` should outlive the run it was sent to (§4.10).
3. ~~**A roll-up on a live team's header**~~ — **built by TD-095 (2026-09-21, PR #389)**: the header reads `host / repo · 2 working · 1 unseen · 1 idle`, counts in the grid's order, *needs you* its ringed mark. In words, not glyphs — the counts sit beside pills that already carry the glyphs.
4. ~~**Exited cards carry less**~~ — **decided otherwise by TD-095** (design §4.5 *The card's anatomy*, Paul 2026-09-21): every card is the same six rows at one height, a dead one included, so a page of cards reads as a grid; what a dead card does carry less of is controls — its foot leads with Forget (PR #384), and a stopped team folds its dead cards away (2026-09-18).
5. ~~**The mode badge**~~ — **built by TD-095 (2026-09-21, PRs #384 and #391)**: *unattended* is a plain quiet word, *interactive* carries the `person` mark at full strength (reserved for it), the toggle moved into *more*, and the Org filter gained *mine*.
6. ~~**The legend**~~ — **built 2026-09-22 (mockups)**: the Org artboard's note gives the sort order as the state pills themselves, glyph and word, and every mockup pill that is a state carries the page's glyph (a due date or a report's status, marked `plain`, does not).

7. ~~**The mockups are behind the page**~~ — **done 2026-09-22**: the Teams strip is gone from `gen.py`; a stopped team is its own card below *No team* — `ao-grind` *wound down 6h ago* with its sessions folded, `cadence-sweep` a definition no session carries — each with **Start**; the pinned control was already gone (TD-095). As first written: `docs/mockups/gen.py` and the generated `Main.dc.html` / `MainDark.dc.html` still draw the Urgent first / Pinned control and the Teams strip, both retired 2026-09-18 (review of PR #229). Regenerate them with the look-and-feel pass rather than twice.

8. **The look-and-feel pass (Paul, 2026-09-18), in progress on a design canvas** — https://claude.ai/artifact/K1y8Nzy3PJnTCecBVANj8y (private to Paul): the Org page, the Inbox page (TD-069) and the state-icon vocabulary, round 1 dark. Constraints Paul has set so far, which bind whatever is built from it: **a state icon must not look like something to press** — the pause bars and the stop square read as buttons, and the glyphs shipped in PR #226 have the same fault (`‖` idle, `■` exited, and `▶` working is a play button by the same reading, all in `app.css`), so they change with this pass (canvas round 2: a prompt caret for idle, a dashed outline for exited, a pulse line for working); **nothing on a page is a control that parses what an agent printed** — Allow / Deny stay the `PermissionRequest` hook's answer channel (§4.2) and nothing else gets buttons from screen text; **the usage chip says what its numbers are** (`grind 19%·49%` is the profile's 5-hour and weekly windows, and reads as nothing); **a light theme is required, later** — so colours land as tokens (`app.css` already defines them on `:root`, with `html[data-theme="dark"]` overriding), never as literals in templates.

**Done when** each idea is either built (its own PR, §4.5a first) or struck here.

**Related:** design §4.5a (**team groups**, **state icon**), TD-069, TD-070, TD-065 (the undefined `chip` class — same page, same kind of finding).

## TD-075: A go-between for what needs the person — answer what is written down, pass up the rest with a recommendation

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-19 (the anchor session; Paul, from the Inbox's needed / steering split — TD-069)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** evaluation
**Status:** Partly done — **designed 2026-09-20 (the anchor; design §4.9b)**, on the shape Paul decided 2026-09-19: a separate role, started per batch of questions. The design in one paragraph: a `techlead:` seat in the team definition and a `techlead` preset with **no grants**; workers' `steer`s and work `ask`s go to it (`{techlead}` in their briefs); it answers cold, an `ask` only from what is written down and with `--source`; everything so answered is filed to the person as *answered for you*, with **Overrule**; the rest is passed up with `--pass-up … --recommend`, keeping the asker and the thread; **the manager fills the seat** when `asks_waiting` leaves zero on an empty one (`ao new --keep-mail`, new: a fresh start otherwise forgets the old record's mail); *who may instruct it* is brief-level and said so; alarms need a person-started techlead holding `alarms`; and **the trial runs with every seat on today's profile** — a cheaper model beside it waits for the agents-only node (TD-077 step 4), as Paul decided. Build order: (1) the seat, the preset, `{techlead}` and the four briefs (`techlead.md` new; grinder, hunter, manager) — after TD-076 step 2, since both edit the same tables — grinder-ao-1; (2) `--source` on a reply, the *answered for you* FYI, and the page's group, row (**Overrule**, **Dismiss**) and header count — the mail half grinder-ao-1, the page half grinder-ao-2; (3) `--pass-up` / `--recommend`, and the passed-up row — the same split; (4) `asks_waiting` (the view, `ao status -v`, the wake digest), **`ao new --keep-mail`** (a fresh start that moves the old record's mail as a resume does — without it a fill forgets the very questions that caused it, §4.1), **`ao inbox --sent`**, and the manager's rule for the seat with its ceiling — a seat is empty or filled, never *finished*, and is not counted in §4.9a's wind-down; `TECHLEAD_WAIT` in the workers' briefs — grinder-ao-1, the repo's own briefs the anchor's; (5) the trial on `ao-grind` (`techlead-ao-1` in `org.yml`, the anchor's, on Paul's word) — **prepared 2026-09-21**: this repo's own briefs carry the techlead's address and the manager's seat rule (`docs/briefs/grinder-ao-1.md`, and so `grinder-ao-2.md`, and `manager-ao-1.md`, in the presets' own words; `{techlead}` reads *none* until the seat is defined), the seat runs the `techlead.md` preset with `context: docs/briefs/techlead-context.md`, and nothing else is needed but the seat in `org.yml` and a team start — **started 2026-09-21 16:56 local, on Paul's word**: `techlead: {name: techlead-ao-1, context: docs/briefs/techlead-context.md}` on `ao-grind` and `techlead: {profile: grind}` under `roles:` in `org.yml` (the file before it: `org.yml.bak-2026-09-21-before-techlead`), then `ao team start ao-grind`. The seat started with the team under `manager-ao-1` as its controller, ran some twenty seconds on an empty inbox and ended — as designed, not a crash — and waits, `exited`, with `asks_waiting` 0. The work given to the members is TD-095's build, chosen because a large build against a fresh design raises real questions; the manager was asked to log each fill and, at wind-down, the totals. **What Paul asked at the start, and the answers, for the record**: the techlead does **not** take over merges — that is TD-093, not designed; a person may put a question to it like anyone, **by mail** (a message, not typing into its pane, which would land in the middle of what it is composing), and an empty seat is filled for a person's question as for a member's; its answers to the person are not filed as *answered for you*; and a person's own interactive session started with `--team` gets the team badge and so the techlead's ear today, and the merge check when TD-093 exists — asked, answered with a source, passed up, overruled, lapsed; fills and their cost; (6) `alarms` and the chain of command — with TD-077 (c), not before its step 4. The *Proposed steps* below are overtaken by Paul's decision (no function-of-the-lead stage); they are kept as the record. **2026-09-21, after #346–#349 merged (grinder-ao-1, PR #360, `src/sessionorc`, for the anchor to merge):** a passed-up entry leaves `asks_waiting`; `wake_digest` carries `bool(asks_waiting)`, so a manager wakes when the count leaves zero and not on 1→2 (the anchor's nit on #348); a sent reply with a `source` stays in its sender's outbox for seven days (`mail.SOURCED_RETENTION`). Still left of step 4: the manager's seat rule with its ceiling, `TECHLEAD_WAIT` in the grinder and hunter briefs, and the `--thread` code step it needs (below). **Where it stands, 2026-09-21 07:14 UTC (grinder-ao-1, at its restart):** step 1 merged (#345), and the seat's wind-down count (#350). **Open, green, reviewed, waiting on the anchor (all `src/sessionorc`):** #346 step 2's mail half (`--source`, the *answered for you* FYI, Overrule routing); #347 step 3's mail half (`pass_up`, `owes_for`); #348 step 4's `asks_waiting` and `ao inbox --sent`; #349 `ao new --keep-mail`. #346 and #347 both add `MailEntry` fields after `handed` in `models.py`, so whichever merges second takes a rebase. Each PR's own ledger text lands with it, including what its review found and left open (copies not forwarded to a resumed successor; `s.view()` of a remote record in some RPC replies; `--keep-mail` across hosts untested). **Left of step 4, once those merge:** the seven-day outbox retention of a sourced reply (after #346); leaving a passed-up entry out of `asks_waiting` (after #347 and #348); the manager's rule for the seat with its ceiling (six fills an hour, then the board) using `asks_waiting` and `--keep-mail` (after #348 and #349); `TECHLEAD_WAIT` (thirty minutes, then the person on the same thread) in the grinder and hunter briefs. **That last one is not brief-only (found 2026-09-21, grinder-ao-1):** `--thread` is refused today for exactly the question it would carry — `agent.py`'s thread branch goes through `_owing_question`, which accepts only the caller's own **answered** question in the **person** inbox (or work the person handed it), so a follow-up on an **unanswered** `ask` held in the techlead's inbox gets *the person inbox holds no question …*. The brief line waits for a code step that lets `--thread` name the caller's own open `ask` to a session, and says what becomes of the techlead's copy (settled so `asks_waiting` stops counting it, and so a late answer from the techlead is not taken over the person's) — that step reads fields #347 and #348 add, so it comes after them, and it is `src/sessionorc`, the anchor's to merge. **Open for the anchor at grinder-ao-1's restart (2026-09-21 ~17:10 UTC), each green or pending CI with its `cadence-review:` comment, none touching this ledger:** #361 builds that step: `--thread` also names the caller's own open `ask`/`steer` to a session, the first closes on every copy as `asked_person`, and `TECHLEAD_WAIT` is in the grinder and hunter templates (the repo's `docs/briefs/` are the anchor's). #362 is the manager template's seat rule with its ceiling, the last of step 4's build. #363 moves the seven RPC replies of *Found in #348's review* to `self._view(s)`. #364 makes a copy follow a resume (*Found in its review* under step 3). As each merges, its note here becomes *done*. A seat on a node still loses its mail on a fill: see TD-057, *Found 2026-09-21*. The page halves of steps 2 and 3 are grinder-ao-2's; steps 5 and 6 are the anchor's and TD-077's. **Page halves, 2026-09-21 (grinder-ao-2):** the *Answered for you* group and row (**Overrule**, **Dismiss**) and the passed-up row's *`<passer>` recommends* line are built (`ui/app.py` `inbox_sections`, `inbox_row.html`; design §4.5a); and the team header's *answered for you* count (§4.5a *team header*; *last opened* is the browser's memory, as FYI's *new* mark is) — the page halves of steps 2 and 3 are done. **Step 1 landed 2026-09-20 (PR #345, Opus, grinder-ao-1):** `techlead:` on a team definition (`name`, `home`, `profile`, `brief`; no `role`, no `grants`), started by `ao team start` after the manager and before the members with `controllers: [manager]`; the `techlead` preset (no lane, no grants, icon `book`, label *Tech lead*) and `techlead.md`; `RESERVED_ROLES` left empty; `{techlead}` filled in every brief with the seat's id, worked out before anything starts (`none` without a seat), and a note from the start if the seat comes up under another id; the rule in the grinder, hunter and manager briefs, and in the manager's a carve-out: the seat is not a worker, so its exit is not a crash, it gets no idle nudge and it is never counted in *every member is finished* (the seat's own fill rule is step 4); `ao team list` and the Org page's card for a team with nothing running name the seat. The techlead brief names the verbs of steps 2–4 and says what to do while `ao` refuses them. **Found for step 4, fixed (PR #350, Opus, grinder-ao-1, 2026-09-21):** `teamrun.wound_down` needed every badged session to have declared, and a seat never declares; it now leaves out the session its definition names as the seat (by name, never by role badge), so a team with a techlead reads *wound down* on the strip and in `ao team list`. **Step 2's mail half: PR #346 (Opus, grinder-ao-1, 2026-09-20; `src/sessionorc`, for the anchor to merge):** `source` on a reply (`ao msg --source`, one line of 200, a session's reply only); the *answered for you* FYI, a `note` from the answerer in the person inbox carrying `answered: {question, asker, answerer, source}` on the question's thread; the reply-path branch sending a reply to such an entry to the asker with a copy to the answerer, `handed` on the asker's copy when the person sends it; `ao inbox` prints both. The page half (group, row with **Overrule** and **Dismiss**, header count) is grinder-ao-2's. **Step 3's mail half: PR #347 (Opus, grinder-ao-1, 2026-09-20; `src/sessionorc`, for the anchor to merge):** the `pass_up` RPC and `ao msg --pass-up <id> --recommend "…" [--answer …]` — the asker's own entry (same id) in the person inbox with the passer's answers, the recommendation first; `passed_up` and `recommend` on every copy; the person's reply goes to the asker copied to the passer and closes every copy; the asker owes the outcome. The passed-up row is the page half, grinder-ao-2's. **Found in its review, not fixed:** `_msg` forwards a resumed record's mail for the addressees it names, never for copies, so a passer resumed before the person answers gets its copy of the answer on its old, closed record (true of every copy today, not only this one) — fix with forwarding over `copies` in `_msg`. **Step 4, first part: PR #348 (Opus, grinder-ao-1, 2026-09-20; `src/sessionorc`, for the anchor to merge):** `asks_waiting` (views, `wake_digest`, `ao status -v`) and `ao inbox --sent`. **Left of step 4:** `ao new --keep-mail` (`create(keep_mail=true)`, `_move_mail` without a resume, scoped to a person or the held record's controllers); a sourced reply kept seven days in the outbox (needs #346's `source`); `asks_waiting` leaving out an entry already passed up (needs #347's `passed_up` — until then a passed-up, unanswered question still counts for the seat); `teamrun.wound_down` leaving out the seat; the manager's seat rule with its ceiling and `TECHLEAD_WAIT` in the workers' briefs. **Found in #348's review, not fixed (older than it):** `rpc_seen`, `set_mode`, `set_stop`, `set_grants`, `set_controllers`, `doing` and `_report` return `s.view()` of a record `_find` may have found on another host, not `self._view(s)`: `id`, `controllers` and now `asks_waiting` come back in that host's framing until the push that follows corrects them. **Step 4, `--keep-mail`: PR #349 (Opus, grinder-ao-1, 2026-09-21; `src/sessionorc`, for the anchor to merge)** — `create(keep_mail=true)` and `ao new --keep-mail`: a fresh start under a name moves the superseded record's mail to the new session by `_move_mail`; refused with a resume, with no record to keep, and from a session not among that record's controllers. **Not built across hosts:** the mailbox is the home's, so a `keep_mail` create routed to a node runs `_move_mail` where no inbox is held; whether the home's copy of the replaced record keeps its mail for the new session is untested, and a seat on a node should be checked before it is relied on. **Step 1b, added 2026-09-21 on Paul's word — a standing context for the techlead, the anchor's:** a techlead starts cold and sees only the asker's framing, and `docs/design.md` is far too large to read per fill, so its brief names a **primer** as its first read — here `docs/briefs/techlead-context.md`, some two to three thousand words: the architecture in a page, the §9 invariants, the questions already decided with where each is written, where things live, the never-list. **It is an index, never a source**: an answer still cites the document it came from (`--source`), so a stale primer can misdirect a search and cannot become an authority; a test holds its section references to ones that exist, as `tests/test_ledger.py` holds the ledger. **And the guidance for every other repo** (Paul: *we will need guidance to other users and agents on how and when to create the techlead primer for their repo*): design §4.9b gains the primer — the team definition's `techlead:` takes `context: <path>`, `ao team start` warns when a techlead seat has none, and `ao team --skill` and the `techlead.md` preset say **when** (before the first team start with a techlead seat; again when the architecture or a standing decision changes — the PR that changes it updates the primer, and the fact-check reads it), **who** (a session of that repo with the design in front of it, or the person — never a session reaching across from another repo), and **what goes in and what stays out** (pointers and invariants in; anything that would be cited as the answer itself out). **Built 2026-09-21 (the anchor): this repo's primer, `docs/briefs/techlead-context.md`, its test `tests/test_primer.py` (sections, invariants, paths and ledger ids must exist), and design §4.9b's paragraph.** **The rest of step 1b built 2026-09-21 (grinder-ao-1, PR #370):** `techlead: {…, context: <path>}` (`org.py`), filled into the seat's brief as `{context}` (`none` without one; `repoconfig.CONTEXT_PLACEHOLDER`), which `techlead.md` now names as its first read — an index, never cited; `ao team start` says, and starts anyway, when the seat has no `context:` or the file is not in its home checkout (`teams._primer_missing`, carried as the start's `notes`, which the Teams strip now toasts too); and the when / who / what-goes-in guidance in `ao team --skill` (*A techlead's primer*). Nothing reads this repo's primer until a seat names it: step 5's trial gives `techlead-ao-1` `context: docs/briefs/techlead-context.md` in `org.yml`. **Two more numbers for the trial, 2026-09-21** — Paul asked whether a techlead should keep a context per asker rather than start cold. The anchor's answer: not for the trial — per-asker context is the anchored reader his own 2026-09-19 decision avoided, it turns one go-between into several that may answer alike questions differently, and on the strongest model each one's context is paid for again after every idle gap; and *cold* is cheaper than it sounds (the primer, the brief and the question are a few thousand tokens) and is not cold where it matters, since a follow-up arrives on its thread and the seat reads its own sent mail first. **So measure it**: how many questions were follow-ups on an earlier thread, and how many times an asker had to ask again because the answer lacked context it would have had. If those are bad, the cheaper cure is a short per-thread digest the seat keeps for itself, not a live context per asker. **Next:** what is left is the trial's measurement.
**Location:** the briefs (`src/agentorc/briefs/lead.md`, `grinder.md`, `hunter.md`, this repo's `docs/briefs/`), `~/.agentorc/org.yml` (a role's `profile:`), `src/sessionorc/mail.py` (a forward that keeps the thread), design §4.10 and §4.8

**Why:** Paul, 2026-09-19: run the grinders on a cheaper model and put a stronger one between them and him — *an executive with a technical lead as the go-between, filtering the items that need my attention, answering those it believes are obvious*. He offered three shapes: a second function of the lead; a new role; or renaming today's lead to *manager* (which is what it does) and giving *lead* to the technical go-between. Optional per team, or on the director.

**PR #361 (grinder-ao-1, 2026-09-21):** `--thread` also names the caller's own open `ask` or `steer` to a session (`_question_to_take_up`): the new question goes to the person on that thread, and the first one closes on every copy as `asked_person`, so the techlead's `asks_waiting` stops counting it and a late answer from it closes nothing. Refused in words for a question already passed up, one already closed, and a `note`. `TECHLEAD_WAIT` (thirty minutes, then the person on the same thread) is in the grinder and hunter templates. The repo's own `docs/briefs/` are the anchor's.

**The anchor's recommendation.**
- **It is a ladder, not a new mechanism.** A worker's question goes to its **controller** first, never straight to the person; each level answers it or passes it up, and the person is the top. Mail already marks who spoke (`[controller]` / `[person]`), so a go-between's answer can never read as Paul's.
- **Start as a function of the lead, with the lead on the stronger profile** — a role's `profile:` is already per role (`org.yml`), so *grinders on Sonnet, lead on Fable* is configuration today. The lead already holds the context the answers need (lanes, members, the ledger). **No rename**: `orchestrator → lead` landed days ago and old records still carry the old badge; nothing keys on a role (§9 invariant 9), so the word costs only churn.
- **Split it out only if the rounds cost too much.** A lead reads status every round; on the strongest model that is the expensive part and the least technical. If it shows, the go-between becomes its own optional role — woken by mail, holding **no `control` grant** (it answers; it acts on no session), which is also the least privilege — and the lead drops back to the cheap profile.
- **What it may answer:** *steering* (a default existed; the worker would have gone on anyway), and a *needed* question **only when the answer is already written down** — the design, the ledger, a brief, a decision of Paul's — **citing where**. Everything else goes up **with a recommendation and suggested answers** (TD-070), so the person's part is one press. **Never**, whatever it believes is obvious: anything destructive, outward-facing, spending, credentials, or a change of scope.
- **Everything it answers for the person is told to the person** — an FYI, *answered for you: the question, the answer, the source* — skimmable and overrulable, with a count on the team's card. The failure this guards against is a confident wrong go-between steering a team all night unseen.

**Paul's decision, 2026-09-19 — one exception to the recommendation above.** *The current lead role does not need a premier model — handling the lifecycle of an agent is something Sonnet could handle. Acting as the go-between needs a model that is higher trust, like Fable, and we don't want to waste its tokens doing grunt work.* So the go-between is **its own role from the start**, not a function of the lead, and the *split it out only if* bullet above is overtaken: lead and grinders on the cheap profile, the go-between on the strong one, woken by mail and otherwise costing nothing. The anchor agrees, and the rest of the recommendation stands (the ladder, what it may answer, the never-list, *answered for you*). What the separate role has to settle, in its design round: **(a) it starts cold** — it does not hold the lead's context, so a question put to it must stand on its own (the question, the options, the asker's default, where it looked), which is the `steer` envelope and TD-070's suggested answers doing their job; **(b) who may instruct it** — mail from a controller reads as instruction, and a cheap-model lead must not be able to instruct the high-trust one on what to answer, so its `controllers` is the person alone, and the lead's part is lifecycle only, through `ao team stop`; **(c) the mail edge** — workers must be able to `ask` and `steer` a teammate that is not their controller; **(d) its context grows all night** and a wake after the cache has lapsed pays for all of it, so its brief keeps answers short and it may be better started per batch than kept; **(e) one account, one usage window** — on the same account as the grinders it is capped when they are, which is exactly when questions pile up; **(f) its name** — not *lead* (taken, and renamed days ago); the anchor suggests **`advisor`**, Paul to choose.

**PR #363 (grinder-ao-1, 2026-09-21):** the seven RPC replies of *Found in #348's review* (`rpc_seen`, `set_mode`, `set_stop`, `set_grants`, `set_controllers`, `doing`, `_report`) now return `self._view(s)`, as `get` and `list` do, so a record on another host comes back as the home addresses it: its id, its controllers, `asks_waiting`, `alarm_to`, and the `unreachable` overlay.

**Paul, 2026-09-19, second round — names, fresh context, and a pause.**
- **Names — decided by Paul 2026-09-19: `manager` and `techlead`, bare `lead` retired** (the rename is TD-076). How it got there: **(Paul's proposal; the anchor's pushback.)** Paul: rename today's *lead* to **manager** (or *lifecycle manager*) — it is what it does — and give **lead** / **technical lead** to the go-between; *advisor* is ambiguous. The anchor agrees with both halves of the meaning and objects to one thing only: **re-using the bare word `lead`**. It is not only a role name — it is the team definition's structural key (`lead:` in `org.yml`, `lead: person`), a column of `ao team`, the glossary's *director > lead > worker*, and the `role` badge on every record started since 2026-09-17. A rename leaves old data readable through an alias (`orchestrator → lead` does this today); a **re-assignment** makes old data *wrong*: a record badged `lead` and a team's `lead:` key would silently come to mean the other session. Recommended: **`manager`** for the lifecycle role (the seat key becomes `manager:`, with `lead:` and `orchestrator` as aliases that warn), and **`techlead`** — shown as *Tech lead* — for the go-between; bare `lead` is retired, never re-pointed. It is a glossary decision (Paul's), and a mechanical rename of the size of TD-055, to be scheduled with this entry and not before the Inbox.
- **Fresh context per question — agreed, and it settles (d) above.** The go-between is **started per question (or per waiting batch) and exits when it has answered**: no context piles up, an idle night costs nothing, and the cache problem disappears. Paul's second reason stands on its own: *composing the message forces the worker to pull the needed context, and may help it make better, less biased decisions* — and the reader is not anchored by the worker's whole narrative. Two things this costs, and their answers: **it forgets its own earlier answers**, so two questions in one night may be answered inconsistently — its brief has it read the team's *answered for you* log first, which is the same list the person skims; and **it sees only the worker's framing**, so its brief has it check the claims in the repo rather than trust the summary. What is new to build: **a seat that is filled on demand** — mail to a role with no live session starts one, under the team's definition — which no part of §4.9 does today.
- **Pause on a steering item — decided by Paul**, and it goes into the Inbox design now (TD-069), not here: see §4.10 and the §4.5a `steer` row.

**Proposed steps:** (1) briefs only, one team: workers ask their lead first; the lead's brief gains the answer / pass-up / never rules and the FYI; lead on the stronger profile. (2) Measure: how many questions, how many answered, how many Paul overruled, what the rounds cost. (3) Only then the product pieces: a forward that keeps the thread and the asker, the *answered for you* FYI as a kind the Inbox groups, and — if step 2 says so — the separate role.

**PR #364 (grinder-ao-1, 2026-09-21):** a copy follows a resume as an addressee does (*Found in its review* under step 3): `_msg`'s forwarding walk (`follow()`, closed record → `superseded_by`, loop-safe) now also runs over the copies, so a passer resumed before the person answers gets its copy on the successor. A forwarded copy is reported in `forwarded` and deduplicated against the sender and the addressees.

**The trial's first run, 2026-09-21 16:56–18:16 local (the numbers from manager-ao-1's wind-down note; its log is `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.log` on branch `manager-ao-1`):** 2 fills (23:24Z and 23:47Z), the seat idle again within about a minute of each; 4 questions put to it, every one a `steer` from grinder-ao-2 building TD-095's page (one a withdrawn test send); 3 answered with a source; 0 passed up; 0 lapsed; 0 taken to the person after `TECHLEAD_WAIT`; 0 follow-ups on an earlier thread; 0 re-asks for missing context. The three answers, read by the anchor: (1) keep *says · `<age>`* under a `doing` line — the design named it twice, and the sentence *nothing on a card carries a second age* was read as being about the state clock and endings, which was the intent and is now what §4.5 says; (2) leave the caption empty under a pending question — the question is the slot's text and the foot rule makes **Focus** the first button; (3) ship `vscode`, `none` and the custom template and **leave the `cursor` preset out**, because §5 makes it conditional on the editor's own documentation and Cursor's documents no such link — a decision the seat took from the design's own condition and cited, which is the shape the role was designed for. All three were right; each carried `--source` and each reached the person inbox as an *answered for you* entry. Two things found: **`--reply-to` with `--pick` cannot carry text** (the manager's friction note; not yet ledgered as its own item), and §4.5's *second age* sentence was loose enough to need the seat's reading — tightened the same day. The per-thread question of *Two more numbers* stays open: no thread saw a follow-up in this run.

**Done when** Paul has decided the shape, and, if adopted, a team has run a night with it and the numbers of step 2 are in this entry.

**PR #362 (grinder-ao-1, 2026-09-21):** the manager template carries §4.9b's rule for the seat, read from `asks_waiting` and filled with `ao new --keep-mail`: `working` → nothing; `idle` with none waiting → close; `idle` with questions waiting over twenty minutes → one send; `exited` or `closed` with questions waiting → fill it under the same name, directory, worktree, profile and brief; with none waiting → left empty. The ceiling is six fills an hour, then the board, and a fill is not a crash restart. This was the last of step 4's build.

**Related:** TD-069 (needed / steering / FYI), TD-070 (suggested answers), TD-072 (mail triage), design §4.10 (mail marks), §4.8 (role presets, `control`), §9 invariant 9, the director brief (`docs/briefs/director.md`).

## TD-077: A caller's identity on one host is a field the caller fills in

**Priority:** High
**Added:** 2026-09-19 (the anchor session; Paul asked whether the mail system stops a bad actor or a rogue agent from spoofing who it is)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** decision
**Status:** Open — **decided by Paul 2026-09-19: B now, and E as the condition for TD-075's trial; C and D are not built; the design says A plainly.** (An earlier reading of a one-character reply as *option 3* was a slip of his finger and was corrected the same day — a decision is not recorded from a reply that does not say what it decides.) **The design landed 2026-09-19**: §4.8a (*Who is calling* — the channel classification, the rule table, identity alarms, the threat model, *observe before enforce*) and §4.4a (*A node that carries no person*). **Live on kmaster in `observe` since 2026-09-19 (#248 promoted the same day; first read: *outside 13 · session:ancestry 2 · no alarms*).** **2026-09-20, with Paul's go-ahead: kmaster's tmux server was restarted under the unit.** Every session was idle or exited; the two live ones were stopped with `ao kill` (both clean: nothing dirty), the cron-started server on the default socket was killed, and the host agent started a new one inside `agentorc-agent.service` — `ao identity` now says *detached-process check on*. (The container node's own tmux server, in its docker scope, was not touched.) One thing the restart showed: the agent computes the check **once** and keeps the answer, so a tmux server that is replaced while the agent runs is not noticed until the agent restarts — harmless here, since the units were restarted after it, and worth a line of code: recompute when the server's pid changes — **built 2026-09-20 (PR #265, a worker):** the tick re-reads the server's pid on a cadence of its own (`ID_RECHECK`, 30 s, one `display-message` in a thread beside the pane list it already takes) and only a pid that moved costs the check itself; a server is **(pid, start time)**, so a replacement landing on the same pid inside one window is not taken for the server it replaced (review of that PR), and no server at all stays *not yet known* rather than *off*. §4.8a says it. **Readings so far** (the tally is in memory and restarts with each promote, so they are kept here): 2026-09-20 13:10 UTC, ~8 h after #251's promote — *outside 268 · session:ancestry 174*, no alarms, no failed check in the journal, a resumed session's hooks and `ao` calls among the 174; no `sid` or `tty` fallback needed yet. Still wanted before `enforce`: a team run, and a node's forwarded calls. Step 3, the anchor's, **not before 2026-09-20 evening**: read `ao identity` and the agent's journal (`identity alarm`) after a day that has seen a team run, hooks, the UI, a person's terminal, VS Code's terminal, the SessionStart hook and a node's forwarded calls; every alarm must be explained — a real forgery, or a legitimate caller the rule gets wrong, which is a fix before `enforce`, never an exception — and only then `local: {identity: enforce}` and a restart. Known before turning: **kmaster's tmux server is in `/system.slice/cron.service`** (it predates the unit), so the detached-process check is **off** there — on the board for Paul, since turning it on means ending every tmux session once. What the build and its two reviews taught, all in §4.8a now: a connection is classified once, at its first request; the on-demand pane list only while a live record's pane is unlisted; a failed check serves as before under `observe` and refuses under `enforce`; `host_files` is no plain read (the red-team read a checkout as the person through it) and a test holds the reads list to *takes no `caller`*; the alarm list keeps the first and counts the rest; and the cgroup clause also needs systemd to have started the agent (CI runs the suite inside a `.service` with the person in it, and so does any agent started from inside an `ao` pane). **Step 1 built 2026-09-19** (this PR: `sessionorc/identity.py` — the classification and the rule table, pure, over a `/proc` reader; peer credentials on `agent.sock`; the one step at the head of dispatch; alarms on the record and the host's own list, coalesced; `whoami`, `ao whoami`, `ao identity`; `local: {identity: …}`; `#{pane_tty}` read with the pane list; the suite runs `off` and `tests/test_identity.py` runs `enforce` against real panes). **Step 2 built 2026-09-19** (a worker, with TD-069 step 2, since both add rows to the Inbox): the host's own alarm list persists (`identity_alarms.json`, mode `0600`, `store.IdentityAlarmStore`, loaded at start, a new alarm written at once and repeats coalesced on the tick — the records' own rule; the tally stays in memory, which is what *since the agent started* means); the card carries a non-pressable **mark** in a token colour, tolerant of a malformed alarm entry (it costs that card its mark, never the grid); the Inbox has a row per record with alarms and one for the host's own list, under *Needs you* and counted, with **Acknowledge** (`identity_ack`, a person's only, refused to every session, deliberately not in `identity.READS`) and the alarms in words, *(others)* read as *and n more distinct claims*; `ao status -v` prints the mode and the detached-process check once for the host; the Org's teams line says *identity: observe* / *identity: off* and nothing under `enforce`. A node's session alarms reach the home on the replica (`identity_alarms` is node-owned, like `tail`) and need nothing new; **Acknowledge on a node's record is routed to that node** (`identity_ack` joined `NODE_ACTS`, review of PR #251), which clears its own list — the home takes the cleared record from the reply, and the node's next report does not bring the alarms back. **A node's own host list is not reachable from the home** — `identity` names no `id`, so nothing routes it over the link — and the home's Inbox shows the home's own list only, which is what §4.8a describes and no more. Build steps: (1) §4.8a in `observe` — peer credentials, the ancestry walk, the classification, alarms recorded and shown, nothing refused — **the anchor's own, with an adversarial review**; (2) **built** — the alarm on the card and in the Inbox, `ao status -v` and the mode on the teams line — a worker;
