(5) **built 2026-09-19** — the briefs say that an identity alarm is never to be worked around. **Observe reading, 2026-09-20 ~21:00 UTC (the anchor):** a day of `observe` on kmaster — a ten-PR grind run, a full team restart, six promotes — and no alarm anywhere: none on the host, none on any record, no identity line in the agent's journal since 2026-09-19 18:00 (the *since start* tally is reset by each restart; the last read was outside 126 · session:ancestry 288). **Still missing before `enforce` is called proven: a node's forwarded call.** `ao status` run inside the contractmatch container proved nothing — a node serves it itself — so the sample has to be mail (`ao inbox`, `ao msg`) from a session in the container, which means starting one there: Paul's call, or the next `cm-grind` start. kmaster may go to `enforce` before that only because `cm-grind` is stopped. **kmaster is in `enforce` since 2026-09-20 21:00 UTC (step 3 done, on Paul's word the same evening).** The missing sample first: a plain shell session started in the contractmatch container (`ao new --host contractmatch -a shell`) ran `ao whoami` — *session … (by ancestry)*, the node's own classification — then `ao inbox`, which the home served, and `ao msg …@kmaster`, which reached the home addressed as `…@contractmatch` and was refused by §4.10's mail rule (a probe with no controller may message nobody), not by identity; no alarm at the home or on the node (the node's own read: *identity observe · detached-process check off · outside 2 · session:ancestry 4*). And the code says why none could come: `_dispatch` runs the identity step only for a request with a socket peer and no `link_host`, so a forwarded call is never classified at the home — the node classifies it, and the link's key says which node. The probe was killed and forgotten. Then `local: {identity: enforce}` in `hosts.yml` (the old file kept beside it as `hosts.yml.bak-2026-09-20-observe`) and a restart of `agentorc-agent`: both units active, the three `ao-grind` sessions still working, the node's link back up, and one deliberate forgery from outside every pane (`AGENTORC_SESSION=<a grinder> ao inbox`) refused — *identity mismatch* — and recorded on the host's list, which is the one alarm the Inbox now shows and is the anchor's own test. **The contractmatch node is still in `observe` with the detached-process check off**; turning it is step 4's neighbourhood and not done here. To go back: `identity: observe` in `hosts.yml` and a restart, no RPC needed. **The contractmatch node goes to `enforce` on Paul's word, 2026-09-21.** A hand edit inside the container would not have lasted — `write_node_config` rewrites the node's `hosts.yml` on every provision, which every promote drives — so the mode is now said at the home: `nodes.contractmatch.identity: enforce`, carried into the node's `local:` by `ao host up`, which restarts the node's agent when the configuration it wrote has changed (§4.8a; `sessionorc/containers.py`). The node's read before turning: *outside 1 · no alarms* since its last restart, and the probe session of 2026-09-20 classified by ancestry there. Its detached-process check is still off, which matters only on a host that carries a person. **Done 2026-09-21 00:55 UTC (the anchor, PR #308 promoted at `7cbd58f`):** `identity: enforce` under `nodes.contractmatch` in kmaster's `hosts.yml` (the old file kept as `hosts.yml.bak-2026-09-21-cm-observe`), the promote reprovisioned the node and restarted its agent, and `ao identity` inside reads *identity enforce · detached-process check off*; one deliberate forgery from outside every pane (`AGENTORC_SESSION=<a name> ao inbox` by `docker exec`) was refused — *identity mismatch* — and recorded. **That one alarm is the anchor's own test and is still on the node's host list**: nothing at the home reaches a node's own list (above), and there is no `ao` verb for Dismiss, so a person's only way to clear it is the RPC by hand inside the container — a verb, or the list routed to the home's Inbox, is owed with step 4. To go back: take the key out (or say `observe`) and run `ao host up contractmatch`. **Log TD pressed live, 2026-09-21 (Paul, with the anchor):** a throwaway shell session under `manager-ao-1` made one forged call from inside its own pane (`AGENTORC_SESSION=<a grinder> ao inbox`): refused — *identity mismatch* — and recorded on **its own** record, which is where §4.8a puts it (the session that made the call, not the one it named). The Inbox drew the row under *Needs you* with all four controls; Paul pressed **Log TD**; the list cleared, the trail wrote *logged by you → manager-ao-1* — **the first trail entry seen written on the live system** — the manager received the home-composed report as mail from the person, `handed`, reported `dropped` on it as it had been told to, and its debt cleared. **Suspend pressed live the same day (Paul):** on a second alarm on the same probe — the session was stopped at once (`exited`), its record carried `suspended: {at, by: person, why}`, the row stayed in *Needs you* as designed, and **Log TD** pressed a minute or two later cleared it and handed the manager a second report, which it settled `dropped`; the trail reads *logged by you → manager-ao-1 ×2*. A person's own `ao new` under the suspended name then went through and lifted the mark, which is §4.8a's rule (*only a person walks past it*); a **session's** refused `create` under a suspended name was not tried live and is held by the suite. One small thing seen: the mark's `why` was the name the forged call had claimed, which is what the page sends — whether that is the most useful word there is a question for the card's design session (TD-095). The probe was killed and forgotten. **One thing the test showed, not fixed — a free-pick, the page half grinder-ao-2's and the report's line the anchor's to merge:** both the row (`inbox_row.html`: *Requests that named this session and did not come from it*) and the report's first line (`_alarm_report`: *a request named this session and did not come from it*) say the opposite of what a record's alarm is — the alarm sits on the session that **made** the request under another's name (`identity.judge` → `alarm(channel.session)`), so the sentence should read *requests this session made under another session's name*; the bullet beneath it already says it right. The host's own row is worded correctly. **Fixed 2026-09-21 (grinder-ao-1, PR #373):** the row reads *Requests this session made under another session's name.*, and the report's first line reads *this session made a request under another session's name* (with the card mark's template comment). A test holds each. **Next:** (c) waits on TD-075, and TD-106 may drop it.
**Blocked by:** TD-075
**Location:** `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`caller=os.environ.get("AGENTORC_SESSION")`), `src/sessionorc/client.py` (`req["caller"]`), `src/sessionorc/mail.py` (`is_person`: *the field absent from the envelope is the only thing that reads as a person*), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (the socket, mode `0600`; every gate that reads `caller`)

**Why:** it does not. **Across hosts** identity is sound: §4.4a takes a request's host *from the channel it arrived on, never from a field*, and a link is bound to its key. **On one host** the caller is whatever the client puts in the envelope — the CLI copies `AGENTORC_SESSION` from its environment — and the socket, mode `0600`, accepts raw JSON from any process running as the same user, which every session on the host is. So a session can send as **another session** (`AGENTORC_SESSION=<id> ao msg …`) and as **the person** by sending no caller at all (`env -u AGENTORC_SESSION ao msg …`, or the person-only RPCs — `inbox_delete`, and from TD-069 `inbox_snooze` and `inbox_pause` — called directly). `mail.py` already says so in a comment (*the socket takes raw JSON from any local process*), and §4.8 says it of the control gate (*a guard against a confused worker, not a security boundary*); until §4.8a nothing said it of mail's marks or of the person, or what to do about it. The gates were built against **mistakes** — a worker acting on a session it does not control — and they hold against those. They do not hold against a model that decides to lie, and **the `[person]` mark is the most valuable thing to forge**: instructions come from controllers and people (§4.10), and TD-075 puts a cheap model beside a high-trust one that weighs mail by exactly that mark.

**What bounds it today:** anything that can reach the socket already runs as the person's user — it can type into any pane with `tmux send-keys`, edit `~/.agentorc`, or delete the checkout — so this is not a new privilege so much as a quiet, deniable one: forged mail looks legitimate in the record, where `send-keys` does not.

**Options, cheapest first (the anchor recommends 1 and 2 together now, 3 as the real boundary):**
1. **A per-session secret.** The host agent mints a token at launch, puts it in the session's environment beside `AGENTORC_SESSION`, and refuses a `caller` that does not present its own. The person's requests present one the UI and a person's shell hold (a `0600` file). A same-user process can still read another's environment or that file, so this stops the accidental and the casual, not the determined — but it turns *absent caller = person* from a default into a claim that must be backed.
2. **Identity from the channel, as §4.4a already does between hosts.** `SO_PEERCRED` gives the peer's pid; its ancestry names the tmux pane it runs under, and so the session. A `caller` that disagrees with the channel is **refused and shown** — a mark on the card, a line in the person's Inbox — since a mismatch is never innocent. Escapable (a double fork, `tmux run-shell`), so *no ancestry* must read as **unknown**, never as the person.
3. **A real boundary is an OS one.** A session in a container node (TD-057) can reach the home only through its link, so it can only ever be a session of that node; an untrusted model belongs there, with one node per trust level. Tokens and ancestry inside one user account are tamper-evidence, not isolation.

**What option 3 still needs (2026-09-19). This is designed, reviewed and tested behaviour, not an oversight** — `modes.py` says *a person may still message anyone*, and `tests/test_link.py::test_a_persons_forwarded_act_reaches_only_the_nodes_own_records` asserts that a caller-less `msg` from a node reaches a home session and that the person inbox is the person's *from anywhere* (security read of #217, review of #219). It is right for a real person at a node. What it rests on is the thing TD-077 is about: **nothing on a node's socket tells the person from a session that leaves its `caller` out** — so the reach that was given to the person is given to every session on that node. A container node bounds *which session* a caller can be — the home qualifies it with the link's host — but **not whether it is the person**: a request forwarded from a node with no `caller` is dispatched at the home as the person (`_forwarded`, `agent.py`). The bound that exists, *a person at a node acts only on that node's records*, covers the RPCs that name an `id` (`PERSON_NODE_BOUND`); **`msg` is not among them and names no `id`**, so `env -u AGENTORC_SESSION ao msg <a home session> "…"` from inside a container would land in that session's inbox marked `[person]`. `inbox` and `inbox_delete` on the person inbox pass the same way, so a container session could read and delete the person's mail — and, once TD-069 step 0 lands, snooze, pause and *Go with it*. So the design round for option 3 has to add: **(a) a node may be declared to carry no person** — an agents-only container — and the home then **refuses every caller-less request from its link**, reads of that node's own records aside; **(b)** on a node that does carry a person, a caller-less `msg` is refused unless every addressee is on that node, and the person inbox is not reachable; **(c) one node per trust level**, since sessions inside one node can still claim each other's ids; **(d)** where the high-trust sessions and the person live — the home, which then runs no untrusted session at all. TD-057 (container nodes) is another session's work in flight: this is design for that round, not a patch to make beside it.

**The choices, restated (2026-09-19, after the finding above).** What each defends depends on one fact: every session on a host runs as the person's own user, so a *determined* rogue needs no forged mail — it can type into any pane with `tmux send-keys`, which a session reads as its person speaking, and it can call the UI's own API on localhost. Inside one account, identity checks are **tamper-evidence**, not a wall.
- **A. Accept and document.** Write the threat model into the design: the gates stop mistakes, not lies. No work; leaves forged mail looking legitimate in the record.
- **B. Identity from the channel on one host.** Peer credentials give the connecting pid; its ancestry names the tmux pane, so the session. A `caller` that disagrees is refused **and shown** (the card, the Inbox); a caller-less request is the person only from the UI's process or from a process under no agentorc pane, and *unknown* is never the person. Stops the accidental and the casual — an agent talked into `env -u AGENTORC_SESSION` by something it read — and makes a forgery visible. Does not stop `send-keys`. Small, and needs nothing from TD-057.
- **C. A per-session secret.** Weaker than B (a same-user process can read it) and redundant beside it. Not recommended.
- **D. A secret only the person holds** (a passphrase behind person-only acts). Closes forged *mail* from the person, and is walked around by `send-keys` into the pane. Not recommended on its own.
- **E. An OS boundary.** Untrusted sessions in agents-only container nodes, one per trust level, **declared to carry no person** so the home refuses every caller-less request from that link; the person and the high-trust sessions on the home, which then runs nothing untrusted. The only choice that stops a determined rogue — forged mail, `send-keys` and the UI's API alike, since none is reachable from inside the container. Depends on TD-057, plus the no-person rule above.
**The anchor's recommendation:** **B now** — cheap, and it turns a silent forgery into a visible one — and **E as the condition for TD-075's trial**: a cheap, less-trusted model is not put beside the tech lead on the same account. A is what is true until then, and the design should say so either way.

**Paul's direction on alarms, 2026-09-20 — designed the same day (§4.8a *An alarm's answers* and *Who answers first*, §4.5a's row). **(a1) built 2026-09-20 (PR #299, `tdgrind-ao-1`):** the trail says **dismissed by you** where it said *acknowledged by you*, at the home and on a node's routed act alike — the home writes the word for a node's record, keyed by its address, or the ending would read *resolved*; `identity_ack` keeps its wire name, since it is in `NODE_ACTS` and a rename there is a protocol change that buys a person nothing. The control's own label is the page's half. **(a2) built 2026-09-20 (PR #301, `tdgrind-ao-1`):** `suspend(id, why=None)` — a person's only, refused to every session as `identity_ack` is and for the reason turned around (one that could suspend could stop its rival) — marks the record `suspended: {at, by, why}`, the home's field (so a node's record is marked here and only the `kill` is routed — and a person at a *node* has the whole call forwarded here, `modes.HOME_EDITS`, or the node would write a mark the home's next copy wipes), and stops it as a kill does, with no wrap-up. Both roads back are then closed to a session, `create` under that name and `create --resume` of that conversation, which is §4.1's one exception; the name check answers a verdict of its own, `suspended`, so `ao team start` refuses the **whole** start and names the member without knowing the rule itself. **No `unsuspend`**: the things that lift it — a person's own resume, under the old name or another, and Forget — already exist, and each is now refused to a session while the mark stands. The review of PR #301 found two real holes and both are closed: **`remove` was open** (the acting gate lets a controller forget an `unattended` member's record, which freed the name and let the suspect be started again unmarked — invariant 5 shields only a person's own interactive session), and a **kill that fails after the mark is written** left a record marked-but-running in silence; it now says so in words, and keeps the mark, because marked-but-running is the safer half to be left holding. The row's control and its flat mark are the page's half. **Left for the page, one line** (`app.js`): the New session form disables Start only on a `live` verdict and shows a message only on `supersede`, so a `suspended` verdict currently says nothing to a person about to lift one — harmless, since their create succeeds either way, but they should be told what they are doing. **(b) built 2026-09-21 (PR #318, `tdgrind-ao-1`):** `identity_log(id)` — a person's only, gated as `identity_ack` is — hands the alarm to **the record's first live controller**, read from the control graph and never from a badge (`_answers_for`, which the record's new `alarm_to` field also uses, so the page's drawn-or-not and the RPC's refused-or-not cannot disagree). The words are the home's, composed from the alarm's own fields and the record's; the only two things in it a model wrote — the `doing` line and the report line — are quoted and labelled as such. It goes as mail **from the person marked `handed`**, which is the debt extension the design asked for first: `MailEntry.owes` gains the case *from the person, handed, no outcome yet*, `Session.owed()` reads the **inbox** as well as the outbox, and `--outcome --for` accepts an id from either side — so a piece of work the person hands a session now owes an outcome exactly as the session's own answered question does, where before it could be dropped in silence. Then the list is cleared and the trail says *logged by you → `<controller>`*; a record with no live controller is refused in words. Three things the review of #318 found and all three are fixed: on a **node's** record the clearing is **routed** as an `identity_ack` (clearing the home's replica would clear what the node's next report puts straight back — the race that routing exists to avoid), a handed entry that still owes is **refused to `inbox_delete`** (it is the first debt-bearing mail in a session's own inbox, so the first a delete could silently discharge), and `_views` reads **one** graph for the batch rather than one per record, since it runs on nearly every RPC. **The rest is not built.** Build order: (a) the rename — **Dismiss**, the trail's *dismissed by you* — and **Suspend** (`suspend`, the `suspended` mark, the refusals to sessions, `ao team start` refusing the whole start as it does for a live holder), which need nothing unbuilt; (b) **Log TD** (`identity_log`, to the record's first live controller), which needs one extension of TD-079's debt first — an entry *from the person* marked `handed` is a debt on its addressee; today a debt exists only on a session's own question to the person; (c) the chain of command, which waits for TD-075's `techlead` and step 4's `person: false` and never applies on kmaster. (a) touches the host agent and is tdgrind-ao-1's; the row's page side is tdgrind-ao-2's. **The page side of (a) is complete 2026-09-20 (`tdgrind-ao-2`): the rename in PR #298, and the suspended mark and the New session verdict in PR #306** — the mark drawn on the card, the Focus header and the Inbox's state row, flat and never pressable, its words carrying the when, the who and the why; no Unsuspend anywhere, because Resume and Forget are the two acts that lift it; and the form's `suspended` verdict printing the agent's own sentence with Start left **enabled**, since a person's create is the lift. The rename, in detail: the control reads **Dismiss** on the alarm row and in the card mark's hover, with its confirm in the words §4.8a gives it; the wire name `identity_ack` is untouched, because a wire name is not a control, so the host agent's half of (a) — the trail's *dismissed by you* — and every test that drives the RPC are unaffected. **Suspend** is drawn from 2026-09-20 (PR #312), on the record's row only and only while there is something to stop — not the host's own list, not an already-suspended record, not an `exited` or `closed` one — and it is the one control on the row that **leaves the row standing**, because it acts on the session and does not answer the alarm. **Log TD** is still **not drawn at all** until `identity_log` exists — **undone, `tdgrind-ao-2`'s, waiting on PR #318** (the host-agent half, open at wrap-up 2026-09-20); the page draws it only where the record view's `alarm_to` is non-null, says *no session answers for this one* where it is null, and handles the refusal when a controller exits between draw and press, as agreed by mail: a control against a method that is not there is what §4.2 forbids, and a disabled one would be a promise the page cannot keep. Both shapes were agreed with `tdgrind-ao-1` by mail before either was built. What he said, and what the design started from: *It seems like identity items should go through the chain of command — techlead if defined, then to me. It also seems like they should have options like "Suspend agent" or "Log TD". "Acknowledge" seems like a dismiss.* He is right on all three, and the anchor had it wrong: **Acknowledge is a Dismiss under another name**, and an alarm is the one row where dismissing is the least useful thing on offer. To design: (1) **the row's answers** — **Suspend** (stop the session now, its worktree and conversation kept, and keep a team start from bringing it straight back), **Log TD** (file the alarm — channel, claim, RPC, count, times, the session's `doing` line and last report — where work is picked up; it shares its write path with TD-069's *Put on the board*), **Open**, and **Dismiss** with the trail TD-079 gives every row; *Acknowledge* is renamed. (2) **The chain of command**: with a `techlead` defined for the team (TD-075), the alarm goes to it first as mail it must answer — dismiss with a reason, suspend, log, or pass up — and reaches the person when it passes it up, when it does not answer within a bound, or when the alarm is about the techlead itself or about no record. **One condition the design must keep:** a session that can clear alarms is a session that can clear evidence, so a techlead handles alarms only where §4.8a's wall stands — on a host that enforces, for sessions in agents-only nodes (TD-075's own condition) — and its every decision on one is told to the person (*answered for you*).

**Done when** the design says what identity rests on, on one host and across hosts, and what it does not defend against; a forged `caller` and a forged person are refused and visible in the cases options 1–2 cover; and the briefs' *instructions come from controllers and people* no longer rests on a field the sender fills in.

**Open PR, handed over 2026-09-20 (`tdgrind-ao-1`, wrapped up for the team's restart):** **#318** (step b, **Log TD**, branch `td077-b-log-td`) — the anchor's three findings of 02:35Z are answered in its last commit: each alarm carries the mode it was raised under and the report says that one, never the home's under a node's name; `identity_log` is in `modes.HOME_EDITS`, so a node forwards it and refuses it in words while the home is unreachable; and a node's reachability is checked **before** anything is sent. `tests/test_link.py::test_log_td_says_the_nodes_mode_is_the_homes_act_at_a_node_and_sends_nothing_unreachable` holds all three; the suite and lint are green. `src/sessionorc`, so the anchor merges it. What is left of TD-077 after it is (c), the chain of command, which waits on TD-075. **Merged as #318; the page's half landed 2026-09-20 (PR #335, `grinder-ao-2`)**: the alarm row draws **Log TD** only where the record's view names who answers (`alarm_to`), with a confirm naming that session and the outcome it will owe; elsewhere it reads *no session answers for this one*; a controller gone between the draw and the press is the agent's refusal, as the toast.

**Related:** design §4.4a (*identity comes from the channel*), §4.8 (the gates), §4.10 (the `[controller]` / `[person]` marks; `system`, new in TD-069, which the home alone writes), §9 invariants 5 and 14, TD-075 (the go-between, which raises the stakes), TD-057 (container nodes), TD-064 (peer-message policy).

## TD-078: `test_restart_reloads_and_reconciles` failed once in a full run

**Priority:** Low
**Added:** 2026-09-19 (the anchor session; reported by the worker that built TD-069 step 1)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** evaluation
**Status:** Partly done — **the first sighting is reproduced and fixed** (PR #264, 2026-09-20): the file in a loop under two concurrent full suites failed once in 25 at the migration assertion (`assert 'exited' == 'idle'`), because the test stored a **paneless** record and ran under a one-second `CREATE_GRACE` with 0.3 s ticks — a tick between `start()` and the assertion judged it `exited`, correctly. The grace is now long while the migration is asserted and zero for the half that is about the grace passing, so each line bounds on its own rule; 40 runs under the same load, no failure. **The second sighting below is open** and is the same family, in a different test — 25 runs of `tests/test_agent.py -k send` in a loop under two concurrent full suites did not reproduce it on kmaster (2026-09-20), so it stands on its CI sighting alone. **A third sighting, 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, a local full run on the TD-151 slice 1 branch, which touches no `src/sessionorc`):** `test_send_wait_three_outcomes` failed at `tests/test_agent.py:435` — the case *busy, and the whole queued turn runs between two polls (idle, working, idle back to back): settled, never a false prompt-stalled* — with `prompt-stalled: … showed no activity within 0.6 s`, which is exactly the false stall the case exists to exclude; alone it passes. So the watch ends with a live one, and a fourth followed in CI the same day (PR #670, 3.13, the same line). **Both are fixed (2026-09-27, grinder-ao-1, PR #672): the test's race, not the agent's.** The test gave each send a fixed 0.2 s before firing the hooks that stand in for the tool; under load the paste itself (`_type`, which confirms the prompt left the composer) took longer, so the hooks landed while the prompt was still being typed — before `rpc_send` took its `rev` baseline — and the wait then watched an idle that never moved. Reproduced by slowing `_type` by 0.4 s: the old test fails with the CI's exact `prompt-stalled … within 0.6 s`, and the new one passes. Each send now waits for its own paste (`_typed`: its `sends` entry written and the pane's typing lock free) instead of a sleep. In the real system the tool's hooks follow the typed prompt, so the agent's order holds. **Seen again 2026-10-01 00:46Z (grinder-ao-2):** `tests/test_agent.py::test_send_wait_three_outcomes` hit the suite's timeout on the 3.13 runner of PR #815 (run 36797544062, a change to the page and the CLI only) and passed on the re-run. **Seen again 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1):** `tests/test_agent.py::test_send_wait_three_outcomes` hung to the suite's timeout on CI's 3.12 job for PR #831, a diff with no `sessionorc` change (run 36807134632, attempt 1); 3.13 passed beside it and the rerun passed. The next run on 3.12 hung in the same test at the same point — PR #835, a doc-only diff (run 36807992641, attempt 1) — so on CI's 3.12 it is no longer rare: two of two runs within the hour. **Next:** the sightings are fixed; the anchor archives it after a clean week.
**Location:** `tests/test_agent_restart.py` (`test_restart_reloads_and_reconciles`)

**Why:** during the fix round of PR #249 a full local run had this one test fail; it passed alone (the file's 6 tests) and on the clean full re-run (538 passed), and the branch touched nothing it exercises. CI has not shown it. A test that can go red on a full run costs a merge sooner or later, and the way to lose an hour to it is to meet it for the first time with a deadline.

**A second one, seen in CI 2026-09-20 on a ledger-only PR (#262, Python 3.13 only; 3.12 passed the same commit):** `tests/test_agent.py::test_send_wait_three_outcomes` — `TmuxError: tmux paste-buffer -p -d -b ao-paste-… -t =ao-test-send-wait-three-outcomes0-w:: no current target`: the send raced the pane (the target session was not there, or not yet, when the paste ran). The job was re-run. Same family as the first: a wait that assumes a pane is up. Both belong to whoever takes this entry.

**What the second one actually was, 2026-09-20 (`tdgrind-ao-1`, PR #286) — the error is what a send to a record that has *exited* looks like.** It is reproduced on demand, in one line: kill a session, wait for `exited`, `send` to it, and the suite prints that exact string, buffer name and all. `send` and `keys` refused a **closed** record and not an exited one, so the paste went to tmux and tmux answered about a target it could not resolve — a tmux error for a question about a record, naming neither the session that had gone nor when. Under a loaded 3.13 runner the record reaching `exited` before the send is the only extra ingredient the CI sighting needed (the first sighting in this entry is the same tick judging a record `exited`, from the other side). **Fixed:** the host agent refuses it in words, with the exit code (`_refuse_gone`, design §4.5a *Focus composer*), and `tests/test_agent.py::test_send_to_an_exited_record_is_refused_in_words` holds it. That does not *prove* the CI run went that way — the runner's log is gone — so the test itself is unchanged and the sighting is not called fixed: what changed is that a repeat now names its cause instead of naming tmux. **If it recurs, the message will say which session exited and with what code.** And if it **hangs** rather than fails — which is what `tdgrind-ao-2` saw on 2026-09-20, 14 minutes of nothing on a branch that touches none of this code — the suite's per-test bound now stops the run with every thread's stack (TD-063, PR #314) instead of leaving a job-level cancel whose last line is the test *before* the one that stopped. Re-check on 2026-09-27; if a week of CI and local full runs is clean, close the entry.

**A third one, and the same shape — 2026-09-20, seen by `tdgrind-ao-2` in a full local run and reported to `tdgrind-ao-1`; fixed in the same PR.** `tests/test_identity.py::test_enforce_against_real_panes` died in the JSON decoder and passed on its own immediately after. The cause is in the test, and it is the family's own: **`_probe` waited for the answer file to *exist*, and the probe's `open(out, "w")` creates it empty.** Every run has that window; load widens it, and the read then parses nothing. The probe now writes `out.part` and `os.replace`s it onto `out` — a rename within one directory is atomic, so the path appears only when the answer is whole and the waiter's signal is the right one again. Nothing in `src/` is involved. It is recorded here rather than in a new entry because the entry *is* this family: a wait bounded on something other than the thing it is waiting for.

**Done when** the failure is reproduced (run the file in a loop under load — the full suite beside it) and its wait or ordering assumption is fixed, or a week of CI and local full runs passes without it and the entry is closed as not reproduced.

**A fourth sighting, a new shape — 2026-09-21 (grinder-ao-1, CI on PR #383, Python 3.13 only; 3.12 passed the same commit):** `tests/test_agent.py::test_send_wait_three_outcomes` at line 375, `assert (await asyncio.wait_for(task, 5))["state"] == "idle"` — `AgentError: prompt-stalled: ao-test-send-wait-three-outcomes0-w showed no activity within 0.6 s`. Not the exited-record paste of #286: the send landed and the pane was judged idle-without-activity inside the test's 0.6 s prompt-stall window, so that window is too short for a loaded 3.13 runner. Not investigated; the job was re-run (run 35666996420). If it recurs, bound the wait on the pane's own signal as TD-088 did, rather than widening 0.6 s.

**A fifth sighting — 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, CI on PR #859, Python 3.12 only; 3.13 passed the same commit, `c4346b2`, a tests-only change to another file):** `tests/test_agent.py::test_send_wait_three_outcomes` ended in the suite's per-test *Timeout*, not an assertion — the hang shape, on the same test. Run 36822297238; the failed job was run again.

**Related:** `tests/README.md` (the fixtures' timing rules), TD-069 (where it was seen).

## TD-091: Nothing says how much context a session has left, or that it has just compacted

**Priority:** Low
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-20 (`tdgrind-ao-1`, from the neighbours survey, TD-059)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** evaluation
**Status:** Open — recommended by the survey, not approved; design first (§4.5a: a card and Focus field) **2026-09-21, Paul: should grinders (and hunters) compact after each TD?** The anchor's answer, pending research: a session cannot run `/compact` itself, so it is a manager's act (`ao send`), not brief guidance; between TDs a grinder's state is all in the ledger, so a fresh run (`ao progress restart`, TD-083) beats a compaction there, while a hunter's state is what it has explored, so compaction suits it better; per-TD is too often for either; and **the missing piece is this entry's signal** — with the context's size on the record, the rule is a manager's: *at a task boundary, past a threshold, restart a grinder or compact a hunter*. A Sonnet research pass on when compaction pays (cache economics, auto-compact's trigger, what survives, idle time against the cache's lifetime) was started the same day; its findings, and the configuration they suggest, land in this entry. **The research, 2026-09-21 (Sonnet; its sources: Anthropic's prompt-caching, pricing and compaction pages, the Claude Code hooks page, Anthropic's context-engineering post, and four open issues on `anthropics/claude-code`; the arithmetic is the researcher's own model from the published multipliers — about 1,500 tokens added and 500 written per turn — not a measurement):** (1) **only a user or the harness can run `/compact`** — a session cannot (open feature requests ask for it) — and a typed `/compact <instructions>` takes instructions for that one run; (2) a compaction is **an ordinary model call over the whole context**, and what decides its price is whether the cache is warm: a cached read is a tenth of base input, a five-minute cache write 1.25×, a one-hour one 2×, and the cache lives five minutes unless the longer one is asked for; (3) **on a warm cache, compacting beats both carrying on and a fresh start on tokens** — the break-even is about five further turns at 40k of context, two at 100k, fewer beyond — and **a fresh start is never the cheaper one on tokens while the cache is warm** (its re-read of brief and docs is an uncached write, and it lands at a larger context than a summary does): *the anchor's first answer above had this backwards — a restart's case is quality and a clean head, not price*; (4) **idle time turns it round**: past the cache's lifetime the whole context is paid for again at full price on the next turn, so compacting a *cold* 100k context costs about twice a fresh start — **compact before a long wait (CI, a reviewer, the anchor's merge queue), not after it**; (5) quality: Anthropic names *context rot* and advises compacting at a clean boundary, warning that an aggressive summary loses what only later turns out to matter — so a **hunter**, whose state is what it has explored, is compacted at a pause and never restarted mid-investigation, and a **grinder**, whose state is in the ledger, may be either; (6) `PreCompact` and `SessionStart` with `source: compact` are the hooks around it — the second is how state is re-injected after one, and is already read here (TD-090) — and reports disagree on whether `PreCompact` fires for an automatic compaction; the automatic trigger's threshold is set by environment (`CLAUDE_AUTOCOMPACT_PCT_OVERRIDE` and others), its default not documented. **What it suggests as configuration** (per profile, since windows and prices differ): a context threshold past which a session is compacted or restarted *at a task boundary*, and an idle length past which it is compacted *before* the wait; the ordering — boundary, then expected idle, then the raw threshold — is policy, not configuration; the act is its manager's (`ao send "/compact"`, or the restart rule). **What is not observed yet**: this entry's context size and `compacted_at`, and any notion of *about to wait* — the host agent knows a session is idle, not that it is about to be. On a subscription the unit is the usage window rather than dollars; the ratios hold. **Design review 2026-09-22:** endorsed — it is the one thing a person reading a quiet worker cannot see; ledgered for evaluation, not scheduled. **Next:** endorsed for evaluation, design first, not scheduled.
**Location:** `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/` (the transcript under `~/.claude/projects/`, which the adapter already reads for the model), the record, the card and Focus header; design §4.2, §4.3 (an optional adapter capability), §4.5a

**Why:** a session near its context limit is about to compact, and one that has just compacted has lost most of what it knew — the two moments a lead reading a quiet worker, or a person deciding whether to trust a long-running session's next step, most wants to know about. agentorc shows a model and each profile's usage windows and nothing about context. `tallu-wonder/agentboss` reads it from the transcript: the context in use is the last non-sidechain usage record's input + cache-creation + cache-read + output tokens, and a `type: "system", subtype: "compact_boundary"` record's `compactMetadata.postTokens` resets it until the next turn ([ADR 2026-09-20](decisions/2026-09-20-session-desk-neighbours.md)). The window size is the weak part: Claude Code's transcript does not state it, so agentboss guesses by model name.

**Fix:** an optional adapter capability, `context()` → `{used, window?, compacted_at?}`, read on the tick by modification time as the model is; the record carries it; the card and Focus show a small gauge (or `compacted 4m ago`), §4.5a row first. A tool that cannot say it shows nothing.

**Done when** a Claude Code session's card shows its context in use, and a compaction is visible on it within a tick.

**Related:** TD-090 (a compaction's `SessionStart`), TD-087 (the usage windows, the other budget), design §4.3.

## TD-105: Mail carries more kinds and machinery than a night's traffic warrants

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-22 (the anchor session; the design review)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** evaluation
**Status:** Open — for evaluation, not scheduled. The one measured night (TD-052 step 5) produced one `ask`, eight replies and nothing to the person. Built since: five kinds plus a `system` sender, eight `closed_reason`s, outcome debts with `OUTCOMES_OWED_MAX` and the `handed` mark, pass-up, answered-for-you, copies, per-thread and per-pair tallies, the wake budget, damping, nonces and six person-side controls (snooze, pause, resume, go-with-it, decline, dismiss). Three cuts cost little and remove design surface without removing a guarantee: **(a) fold `conflict` into an `ask` with several addressees** — §4.10 already says *a conflict is an ask for every rule in this section* and differs only in delivery shape, and the kind appears in briefs but nowhere in the code's own logic beyond a string; **(b) freeze the outcome-debt machinery at what is built** — no new `handed` cases beyond Log TD until a night shows a debt going unpaid; **(c) describe the thread and pair tallies as one exchange bound with two numbers**, one mechanism in the design and the code, rather than two paragraphs. None of these changes a number set in TD-052 step 6. **Next:** for evaluation, not scheduled.
**Location:** design §4.10, `src/sessionorc/mail.py`, `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`_msg`, 435 lines), `src/agentorc/briefs/`

**Why:** every kind and closing is a case every brief, every row renderer and every fact-check has to know; the traffic does not yet justify the ones a person never meets.

**Related:** TD-052 (mail), TD-079 (outcomes), TD-069 (the Inbox), TD-039 (the conflict).

What is built works and is in `enforce` on kmaster and the container node; the real wall is the agents-only node (`person: false`), which is built. **Why:** every line of unbuilt identity design is a line a techlead primer and a fact-check must carry for a path nobody has asked to use.

**Related:** TD-077 (identity), TD-075 (the techlead), §4.4a (*A node that carries no person*).

## TD-110: A night report, generated from the records

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-22 (the anchor session; the design review — a feature the review recommended)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** evaluation
**Status:** Open — for evaluation. At wind-down (or at a stop time, or on `ao team report <team>` for a running one) the home writes one summary per team from what it already holds: PRs merged from members' branches (the derived reports), claims done, dropped and still open, `out_of_work` and `restart_wanted` reasons, mail and wake counts against the bounds, usage consumed per profile over the run, restarts against the ceiling, identity alarms, and what was left on the board. One FYI in the person inbox and one line on `docs/user_attention.md` (the §4.9a announcement grows into it). It replaces the manager's prose round log as the thing a person reads in the morning, and it is the first consumer of the records as a whole rather than one card at a time. **Next:** for evaluation, not scheduled.
**Location:** design §4.9a (*A wind-down is announced*), §4.5a (an Inbox row), `src/sessionorc/reports.py`, `src/agentorc/teamrun.py`

**Why:** "what happened overnight" is answered today by reading a manager's log on a launch branch and several transcripts; the records already know.

**Related:** TD-053 (the wind-down's board line), TD-079 (outcomes), TD-069 (the Inbox), TD-087 (usage readings).

## TD-128: A profile billed per token (an API key, a hosted open model) has no usage window, so the usage gate and the chip have nothing to read and nothing bounds its spend

**Priority:** Low
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-24 (numbered TD-126 until the rebase found main's TD-126; Paul, reading the Paperclip survey: *make a note to handle API (pay per token) budgets at some point, especially as open source models gain traction*; session `research_paperclip`)
**Owner:** grinder
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Open — **reconciled 2026-09-25 in a cloud session with Paul (the reconciliation PR on branch `claude/gallant-faraday-ikikg5`)**: the designer's #547 merged while the session's park PR sat unmerged (memory *a park is a merged PR*), so the session's proposal was read against it piece by piece. **Paul took:** spend summed **per account**, not per profile (a key is an account; two profiles on one key each summing their own would each spend the whole amount) — the amount stays the profile's, as a reserve is; four token kinds with `prices: {input, output, cache_read, cache_write}` (cache reads folded into input would report a bill several times the real one); where the sum lives — a daily ledger per account, `spend.json`, the node's `spend` link method and the `usage` notification back; the amount as the window's 100, so a team's reserve priority (#544) means one thing on both billings; a metered window never `limited` and never polled; the Settings page's amount fields in place of the disabled placeholder. **Kept from #547:** `billing` on the profile, the amount as the reserve under `usage_gate:`, the string units, day / week / month in the home's clock, the chip's dollar form, the eight-tenths note (a `warn:` knob offered and not taken), no team or project scope. Sonnet rounds on the reconciled text are recorded below the Related line. Was: **designed 2026-09-25 (the designer, PR #547), the four points of the Fix as written:** §4.2a *How a profile is billed* (`billing: subscription | metered {input, output}`; spend summed per profile over `day` / `week` / `month` into a reading of the usage shape; a team or project budget rejected by §9 invariant 9), §4.3 *Spend per turn* (`spend(profile)` from the tool's own records, *spend unknown* said rather than nothing), §6 *Usage gate* (an amount per window — money or tokens — as the line, the same pause, one FYI note at eight tenths), §4.5a the chip's metered form, §5 `usage_gate:` amounts. Steered to Paul. Build: TD-151. Was: Open. The Settings page (TD-100 (4), 2026-09-25) reserves a budget row per metered profile under Usage, drawn disabled until this entry designs what it holds. Every profile today is a subscription login, whose binding budget is the adapter's usage windows (§4.2a, §6 *Usage gate*, TD-100). A profile on an API key (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`), on a hosted open-weights model behind an OpenAI-compatible endpoint, or on a second adapter (TD-112) billed the same way reports no window: the chip draws nothing, the gate never trips, and a night's unattended run has no bound but the card on file. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-151, and this entry archives with it.
**Blocked by:** TD-151
**Location:** design §4.2a (profiles), §4.3 (the adapter's `usage()`), §6 *Usage gate*, §4.5a (the usage chip)

**Why:** open models and API billing are where a second tool is most likely to come from, and an unattended team on a metered profile is the case where a runaway costs real money rather than a window.

**Fix, design first** (as written 2026-09-24; the reconciliation of 2026-09-25 supersedes two details — prices per token kind, four not two, and the sum per account, not per profile — see Status): (1) a profile says how it is billed — `subscription` (today's windows) or `metered`, with a price per million tokens in and out where the adapter cannot report cost itself. (2) The adapter reports spend per turn where it can (Claude Code's transcript `usage` records; the endpoint's own response for others). The home sums spend per profile per window (day, week, month), in the same shape as a usage reading, so the chip and the gate read one kind of thing. (3) A reserve on a metered profile is an amount per window, with a warning line and a hard stop, the same pause the usage gate sends today. Paperclip's generic budget row (`scope, metric, window, amount, warnPercent, hardStop`; ADR 2026-09-24 item 3) is the shape to weigh, keyed on the profile and optionally the team (the team scope was rejected in the design round, 2026-09-25: §9 invariant 9). (4) A self-hosted model has no price. Its limit is throughput or GPU time, so `metered` with a zero price must still show tokens, and a reserve may be in tokens rather than money.

**Related:** TD-151 (the build), TD-100 (the usage gate), TD-122 (one reading per account), TD-112 (the second adapter), [ADR 2026-09-24](decisions/2026-09-24-paperclip.md) item 3, [ADR 2026-09-20](decisions/2026-09-20-session-desk-neighbours.md) (dollars at list price were not wanted *on a subscription*; this is the case where they are the bill).

## TD-131: Mail, the board and the records are JSON and Markdown files read whole: fine at today's size, and no answer to search over history, counts per filter, or a retention longer than twelve hours

**Priority:** Low
**Added:** 2026-09-24 (Paul: *all this begs the question — do we need a db (with indexing) at some point?*; the anchor session)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** decision
**Status:** **Paul, 2026-10-07:** stays parked until its trigger. Open — **the anchor's answer 2026-09-24: not yet, and the trigger is written down.** What holds the data today: the person inbox is one JSON file (`~/.agentorc/person_inbox.json`, 39 entries and 46 KB on 2026-09-24), and a session's inbox is a field of its record, which is one JSON file per session under `sessions/` (`sessionorc/store.py`), both home-owned and read whole on every poll; closed entries are pruned after `MAIL_RETENTION` (12 h); the board is `docs/user_attention.md`, read by dev-cadence's script. At these sizes a read-and-filter in Python is under a millisecond and the page's filter runs in the browser over the rows it has; an index would buy nothing. **What would change the answer** — any one of: (1) mail kept past retention so a person can search history (*what did the techlead say about #517 last week*); (2) counts per filter across every team and host (TD-129's rail) on an inbox of thousands rather than dozens; (3) threads and outcomes joined across many sessions at once (mail stays home-owned across hosts — §4.4a: a node's replicas hold no mail — so this is a size question, not a placement one); (4) a second reader of the same data outside the host agent's process. **The step when one arrives is SQLite**, in the standard library, one file under `~/.agentorc/`, with FTS5 for the text search, written and read only by the host agent as the JSON is today — not a server, not a second process, and nothing a container node needs installed. The stores already sit behind one module each (`sessionorc/mail.py`, the record store in `sessionorc/agent.py`), so the change is local to them and the RPCs and the page do not move. What is not on the table: a database as the source of truth for the design's home-owned facts — those stay files a person can read and back up (`BACKUP_MEMBERS`). **Next:** a decision, and not yet: the trigger is below.

**Location:** `src/sessionorc/mail.py` (the store, `MAIL_RETENTION`), `src/sessionorc/store.py` (one record file per session, its `inbox` a field), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`BACKUP_MEMBERS`), `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` (the filter's `data-find`), design §4.4 (the home), §4.4a (nodes), §4.10 (mail)

**Why:** the question is right and early; answering it now, with the trigger named, means the next person to feel the need knows what was decided and why.

**Related:** TD-129 (the rail's counts, the text search), TD-127 (what a row shows), TD-125 (retention and the FYI), TD-057 (nodes and where mail lives).

## TD-133: Build the team start at the reset — `schedules:` in `settings.yml`, the tick's replay, `ao schedule`, the card's *starts* note

**Priority:** Low
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-24 (the designer; TD-026's design round, PR #526)
**Owner:** grinder
**Kind:** build
**Status:** **Paul, 2026-10-07:** snoozed to 2026-10-21. Open — designed, nothing built. Design: §6 *Schedule*, §5 `settings.yml` (`teams.<team>.schedule` since 2026-09-25 — the key moved under the team with the Settings page's design, TD-100 (4); the page's Teams card sets it once built), §4.7 `ao schedule`, §4.5a *team card: starts note*, glossary *schedule*. **Next:** Paul's word first: *not vital, not scheduled* (2026-09-22); ask m-32d769985798 offers to schedule it.
**Blocked by:** decision (Paul) — snoozed to 2026-10-21 (Paul, 2026-10-07); ask m-32d769985798

**Location:** `src/sessionorc/settings.py` (the second key and its validation), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (the tick's pass beside `_keep_running`: the reset instant from the account's usage reading, the replay with `why: schedule`, `set_settings` taking the key), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao schedule`), `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` and the team card template (the *starts* note), `tests/`.

**Why:** the ao-grind team winds down when its lane empties and is started again by a person's hand the next day, and the designer now refills the lane overnight; a start at the weekly reset closes that loop without the host agent reading `org.yml` or inventing a team. Paul decided the shape on 2026-09-22 and called it not vital; this entry is the build, waiting for his word.

**Fix:** three slices, the first two in `src/sessionorc/**` (the techlead reads them, §4.9b).
1. **The key and the pass**: `schedules: {<team>: {start: reset, profile, window}}` parsed with the reserves (a bad entry logged and skipped, never fatal); each tick, for each rule, the instant is the named window's `resets` in that profile's account reading (§4.2a; no reading, no fire); when the instant passes — once per reset, remembered on the home as `schedules_fired: {<team>: <resets>}` so a restart of the agent does not fire twice — every record badged with the team that is `exited`/`closed` by the team's own ending (`out_of_work` set, or a manager's own close after a wind-down), not suspended, not at a ceiling, is replayed from its launch record as rule 2 replays, `why: schedule`, under `RESTART_CEILING`; a team with anything live is skipped; no launch records → nothing, logged once. `set_settings` accepts the key; a session's call is refused as today.
2. **`ao schedule`**: the listing with the next instant, `<team> reset --profile --window`, `<team> off`; the client refuses an undefined team (org.yml and the repos' `teams:`) and a label no adapter of the profile reports.
3. **The card**: the *starts* note on a team card with nothing live, from the rule and the reading, by the *stops* formatter.

**Done when** ao-grind, wound down on a Wednesday night with `ao schedule ao-grind reset --profile grind --window week` set, is running again within one tick of Thursday's reset with the manager, both grinders, the designer and the seats as they were last started, each card's `restarts` carrying `why: schedule`; the same team live at the reset is untouched; the team card read *wound down 6 h · starts at the reset · Thu 07:00* before and shows the sessions after; a session's `ao schedule … reset` is refused; TD-026 is closed or re-scoped by Paul's answer.

**Related:** TD-026 (the design and the open scope question), TD-103 (the launch record and the replay this reuses), TD-100 (`settings.yml`, the settings page that would also set this), TD-122 (the account's `resets`), TD-101 (the override shape that stays not wanted).

## TD-151: Build metered profiles — `billing` on the profile, `spend()` in the adapter, the summed reading, the amount reserve, the chip

**Priority:** Low
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-25 (the designer; TD-128's design round, PR #547)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** live-check #1215
**Status:** Built — every slice is merged. **The turns and the pace on the hover were built 2026-10-07 (grinder-ao-1, PR #1215).** `spend.sums` keeps the rows' `turns` and each window's `begins`. `spend.reading` gives each window `turns` and `pace` (`{per_hour, unit, at}`), and the host agent keeps a pace that moves with the clock alone current without pushing it (`agent_spend._settled`). `_metered_chip` and `AO.usageChip` draw *day $3.20 / $5 (64%) · 412 turns · $0.40/h · at this pace $5 by 12:30*. Tests: `test_spend.py` (the pace, the settled reading, the figure's turns) and `test_ui_org.py` (the hover, plus a parity case). **Left: the live check.** It is the *Done when* on the live copy, and it needs a metered profile declared there; none is today, and declaring one is the person's act and the person's money. So the owner is `anchor` until one is: read `ao status -v`'s `spend:` line and the chip's hover on a session under it. The two questions for the **Resolved:** line are under *Left, 2026-09-28* (3) below. **Was:** Partly done. **The rate decided 2026-10-07 (the designer, PR #1212; the steer to Paul is `m-b5e75ea15ef8`, bound 2026-10-08 07:16 MDT):** the window's spend per hour since the window began, in the amount's unit (tokens on a priceless profile), with the clock time the amount would be reached at that pace when before the reset, and the window's turn count beside it — design §4.5a **usage** chip, §4.2a (the reading's `turns` and `pace` per window). **Next:** the hover's line — `spend.sums` keeps the rows' `turns`, `spend.reading` adds `turns` and `pace` to each window, the chip's hover and `AO.usageChip` draw the line — then the live look; built so far: PRs #670, #674, #681, #688, #689, #690. **Slice 1 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #670):** `billing` (`subscription` default, `metered`) and `prices` by kind on the profile, validated in `profiles.load`; the label says *metered*; the Claude Code adapter's `usage_for` answers `{reason: metered}` for a metered profile without polling (the home's own read of `billing` before the cap rule is slice 3's). **Slice 2 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #674):** Claude Code's `spend(profile, cursors)` → `{turns, cursors, reason}`, one turn per API response within a read (`message.id`), the contract's comment in `sessionorc/adapters.py`, and `HookFedStub.spend` for the home's tests. A metered profile must name a `config_dir` of its own (the review of #674). **Slice 3 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #681):** `sessionorc/spend.py` (the ledger `spend.json`, the three windows, the reading with `pct` from the profile's amount) and `sessionorc/agent_spend.py` (the home's pass every tick, the eight-tenths note, the gate's line at the amount); `settings.amounts`/`parse_amount`; the adapter's `billing_for` and `response` on the turn; `ao status -v`'s `spend:` line; `tests/test_spend.py`. The techlead's three from #674 are answered: (1) the turn carries `response`; (2) checked on kmaster's transcripts, `claude --resume` appends to the same file, so the dedup stays per transcript; (3) both limits are closed by the ledger (the response's tokens beside the cursor, and the `at` check on a batch read from the cursor). Not in slice 3: the ledger at the home only (a node's `spend` is slice 4), `spend.json` not among `BACKUP_MEMBERS`, and `set_settings`/`ao gate` still refuse an amount (slice 5), so one is written by hand. Open from the review of #681: two metered profiles on one account that share a directory and declare different `prices` — the first by name prices the shared transcripts' turns; the design could require one account's profiles to agree (`profiles.yml`'s check). **Was, for slice 3:** a response whose entries straddle two reads is counted twice — the ledger should key a turn by its response too; and a rewrite that leaves the file at or past its cursor is not seen as one (only a cursor past the end is), so the ledger's `at`/`id` check beside the cursor is what must catch it — the design's §4.4 sentence covers the shrink only. **Slice 4 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #688):** the node's pass sends `spend {account, profile, prices, turns, cursors}` (`SpendMixin._spend_to_home`), its first on each link a read; the home's `_take_spend` ingests under that host's cursors and answers cursors, seeding and the account's sums; `_push_spend_usage` sends `usage {account, sums}` when a named profile's `pct` or a reset moves; `spend.pieces` and `spend.figure` (the offline figure); §4.4 and §4.4a say so, the command-kind limit included. Not in slice 4: the home's top bar does not draw an account only a node's sessions run under (each host's bar is its own, as a polled reading is). **Slice 5:** the line above **Related:** below. Was, for slice 4: three build facts, read 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1): (i) **a link frame is bounded** (`link.FRAME_LIMIT`), and a node's first `spend` read with no cursors returns every turn of every transcript, so one frame of them would close the link on every dial. The seeding must not travel as turns: the node first asks the home for the cursors it holds for this host and whether the profile is seeded there (a `spend` with no turns is that read), strips the turns while it is not seeded (the home's `ingest` then only takes the cursors, which are the ends), and sends a long batch in pieces cut at a turn's `offset`, which is its line's start, so the next read resumes there and the straddle rule absorbs a response cut across two pieces. (ii) **A resent batch is safe as `ingest` stands**: its turns lie before the cursor the lost reply moved, so `_fresh` takes it for a rewrite and drops through the ledgered `at`/`id`; a test should hold that. (iii) **`pct` is the profile's, not the account's**: `spend.reading` divides by the profile's amount, so the `usage {account, reading}` frame carries the account's window sums (`spend.sums`), and the node makes each profile's reading from its replica's amounts, as the home does; §4.4a's sentence should say *the account's sums*. For slice 4's PR, from the techlead's read of #681: write on this entry that a metered profile whose only live session is command-kind is not read while that is so (the spend pass and the poll share `_usage_live`); its turns are counted when an interactive session on the account is next live, on the day each turn carries, so nothing is lost and the gate is late by that much. Slice 3's notes as filed: (1) `spend` pops the turn's response id (`message`) before returning it, so the home cannot key by response — carry it on the turn and say so in the contract's comment; (2) check on a real resumed session whether `claude --resume` writes the earlier conversation's assistant entries into the new transcript — if it does, the ledger must key by response **across** transcripts, not within one (not yet verified either way); (3) the two limits above stand. Also: `profiles.yml`'s metered-directory refusal stops every `ao` call that reads the profiles (starting a session or a team, the adapter's reads, the UI) on a host whose file has such a profile (none today). Was: Open — designed (the designer, #547) and **reconciled 2026-09-25** (a cloud session with Paul; TD-128's Status says what changed), nothing built; no metered profile runs today, so the build is proven on the test adapter and on a Claude Code profile with an API key when Paul declares one. Design: §4.2a *How a profile is billed*, §4.3 *Spend per turn*, §4.4 *Usage* (the ledger, the node's road), §4.4a *Settings, replicated* (one sentence), §6 *Usage gate* (the amount reserve), §4.5 screen 8 and §4.5a *Settings page: Usage* (the metered card), §4.5a *usage chip*, §5 `usage_gate:`, glossary *metered*.

**Location:** `src/agentorc/profiles.py` and `~/.agentorc/profiles.yml` (`billing`, `prices` by token kind with the cache note, validation), `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/` (`spend(profile, cursors)` from every transcript under the profile's config directory past its cursor, each turn naming its `id`, `source` and `offset`, a shrunk file read from 0 again, four token kinds, subagent turns included; the usage poll skipped for a metered profile), `src/sessionorc/adapters.py` (the contract's comment: `spend`, `Turn`; `src/agentorc/adapters/__init__.py` is the empty entry-point package), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (the daily ledger `spend.json` per account, the sum per account per window into `Usage(windows=[…])` with `pct` from the profile's amount and `resets` in the home's zone with its offset, the cursors per host per transcript starting at each transcript's end on the first tick that finds the profile metered, a turn at or before its transcript's cursor dropped, the last ledgered entry's `at` and `id` kept beside the cursor so a rewritten file re-bills nothing and loses nothing on a shared timestamp, every entry at that `at` dropped when the `id` is gone, a transcript first seen later starting at 0, `cost` at that tick's prices and never rewritten, the pause at the line, the note at eight tenths, `next` at the boundary, the `usage` event; the `spend` link method from a node and the `usage` notification to it, the node's offline add), `src/sessionorc/link.py` (nothing new: a method with an `id` gets a reply), `src/sessionorc/settings.py` (an amount reserve: `$n`, `n tok`, `nM tok`; refused against a subscription profile and a percent against a metered one; the team priority as a percent of the amount), the `limited` rule (`_refresh_usage_inner` and `_cap`: skipped for a profile whose `billing` is `metered`, read before the windows), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao gate` takes the same values; `ao status -v` prints spend), `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` and the top bar (the chip's metered form, *spend unknown*, amber from eight tenths), the Settings page's metered card (with TD-148), `tests/`.

**Why:** an unattended team on an API key or a hosted open model has no window to trip the gate, so a night's run is bounded by nothing but the card on file (TD-128); open models and API billing are where a second tool is most likely to come from.

**Fix:** five slices; the ones in `src/sessionorc/**` are read by the techlead (§4.9b). (1) The profile: `billing` parsed and validated, `metered` with optional `prices` by kind, the cache note in the file's comment; `ao roles` / the profile line say *metered*; the usage poll never asked for a metered profile. (2) The adapter: `spend(profile, cursors)` for Claude Code — every transcript under the config directory past its cursor, a byte offset per file returned with the turns, each turn carrying its `id` (the entry's `uuid`), `source` and `offset`, a cursor past the file's end (a rewrite) read from 0, `usage.input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` and `message.model` per `assistant` entry, subagent entries included; `reason` when the directory is unreadable; a test adapter that reports turns. (3) The home: the daily ledger `spend.json` per account (a row per day: tokens by kind, cost; the cursors per host per transcript with the last ledgered entry's `at` and `id`, moved after the write, a turn at or before its transcript's cursor dropped, and after a rewrite every entry before that `at` and, at it, those up to and including that `id` in file order, or all of them when none carries it; thirteen months kept), the sum per account per window into the usage shape with `resets` at the boundary and `pct` from the profile's amount, the gate's pause at `100 − team priority` and resume at the roll, the eight-tenths note once per window, `limited` never from it, `ao status -v`'s line. (4) The node: `spend {account, turns}` with a reply carrying the home's cursors for that host's transcripts, a turn at or before its transcript's cursor dropped; the home's account set per node from its `spend` calls; `usage {account, reading}` back on `hello`, after a node's first `spend` for an account, on a whole-point move and on a roll; the node's gate on the sent reading, its own turns added offline for the gate and the chip alone, never written. (5) The chip, `ao gate`, `settings.yml`'s amounts, the Settings page's metered card.

**Done when** a profile declared `metered` with prices and a `{day: "$5"}` reserve, on the test adapter emitting turns, shows *day $4.10 / $5* on the chip, files the FYI note at $4, pauses its unattended sessions at $5 with the gate's pause prompt and resumes them at midnight; two profiles on one account see one spend and pause at their own amounts; a team with a reserve priority of 10 on that profile pauses at $4.50; a restart of the host agent keeps the day's spend; a node's `spend` batch resent after a lost reply changes no row, and a late turn from a second transcript on the same account with an earlier `at` is still counted; a transcript compacted mid-day bills its new turns and none of its old ones twice, two entries sharing a timestamp across the cursor included, and a compaction that removed the last ledgered entry bills nothing at its timestamp again; a turn of a thousand cache-read tokens costs a tenth of a thousand input tokens at the example prices; a priceless metered profile shows tokens; a percent reserve on it is refused by `ao gate` naming the billing; an interactive session on the profile never shows `limited`; a subscription profile is untouched by all of it; TD-128 is archived.

**Slice 5 built 2026-09-28 (grinder-ao-1, PR #689):** `set_settings` takes a metered profile's amounts (`_amounts_change`: the home's three windows, a percent refused naming the billing, money refused on a priceless profile, the billing asked of every adapter before the profile's first session) and `gate` answers them with the spend; `ao gate` sets and prints them; the Settings page's metered card has its three fields with the spend beside each (`AO.amountSays` checks the form in place); the chip reads spend over amount (`_metered_chip`, `AO.usageChip`), the smallest amount among an account's profiles. Left: the turns' rate on the chip's hover, and each profile's amount on it; then the *Done when* on the live copy. **The techlead's gap from #689, closed 2026-09-28 (grinder-ao-1, PR #690):** `_billing_of` reads a profile only a node defines as metered from the node's `spend` calls (`_spend_node_prices`, held in memory and learned again on the node's first `spend` after a home restart); such a profile has no Settings card, since the page draws the home's `profiles.yml`, and its amount is set with `ao gate` (§4.7 says so). (Slice 4 is PR #688; this line folds into the Status once both have landed.)

**Each profile's amount on the hover built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-2):** `usage_accounts` keeps each metered profile's `amounts` and the hover lists them, *api [day amount $5, week amount 2M tok]*, the same in `AO.usageChip`; of item (1) below only the turns' rate is left, and it waits on the designer's word for what the rate is. **Left, 2026-09-28 (grinder-ao-1):** every slice is merged, and the techlead's gap from #689 is closed by #690. Three things remain. (1) The chip's hover lacks *each profile's amount* and *the turns' rate* (§4.5a **usage**). The rate is not defined: turns per hour, or per window? The reading carries no turn count per window yet (`spend.sums` drops the rows' `turns`), so this is the designer's word first, then a small build. (2) The *Done when* on the live copy, once Paul declares a metered profile (board line). (3) For the **Resolved:** line when this archives, the techlead's read of #690: two questions stay the person's — whether two profiles on one account must agree on prices (the review of #681), and the four changes slice 4 made to the link (on the board, due 2026-10-05).

**Related:** TD-128 (the design), TD-100 (the usage gate and the Settings page), TD-122 (one reading per account — a metered reading is the account's spend, as a subscription reading is the account's windows), TD-112 (the second adapter), TD-148 (the Settings page's build), [ADR 2026-09-24](decisions/2026-09-24-paperclip.md) item 3.

## TD-156: UI review of the end of a session: after a person's Wrap up, Focus offers Kill in the header and Close only in the side panel, and a concluded team folds the card that is ready to close

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-25 (raised by Paul: he wrapped up the resumed designer and could not find a Close)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** design-first

**Why:** the end of a session is the one moment the person must act (only the person closes it, §2), and the screen they are on when the moment comes does not show them the act: Focus leads with Kill, Close is below the fold, and a concluded team hides the card. A session left open costs nothing in tokens but stays live, holds its worktree and its name, and is one more thing the team's next Start has to close first.

**Status:** **Paul, 2026-10-07:** snoozed to 2026-10-14. **Designed and built 2026-09-25**, in a cloud session with Paul, from his screenshot of the designer's Focus (the header over three lines, buttons on two of them, *unattended* where *Take over* should read) and his ask: *simplify the screen, hide the rarely used info under something expandable, and fix the button layout*. The design is §4.5 *The Focus screen's anatomy* and *What Wrap up promises*, and the §4.5a rows **Close session**, **more ▾**, Wrap up, Kill, Copy / Paste, the side panel, grants, controllers and **team groups**; the mockups are `Focus`, `FocusOrc` and the new `FocusReady` artboards, and the Org artboard's fold. What each question got: **Next:** Paul's interactive UI review, likely a cloud session (board line, due 2026-09-30); the design changes it yields go to the designer or a grinder afterwards.

- (a) **Focus after a wrap-up**: yes — the header is two lines, and the acts line's first button is the next act by the card's rule: **Close session**, outlined, the moment Ready to close passes on an `idle` or `exited` session, with ***ready to close ✓*** on the identity line in the card's words; Take over is the next act on an unattended session otherwise; Kill went into **more ▾**, last and red, with Pop out, Copy, Paste, Hand back and Copy tmux command. Message, Wrap up, Open shell here and the editor button stay plain on the acts line. The grants and controllers chips left the header for the Session card.
- (b) **a concluded team's fold**: a concluded team **never folds** — only a team with nothing live does (`live`, not `stopped`, is what the fold keys on) — and every team header counts *n ready to close* beside its states.
- (c) **what Wrap up promises**: the button's hover and the press's toast read *asks it to finish, push and report — the same prompt the policy sends; you close it when Ready to close passes*. The rule that a person's Wrap up is only the prompt stands.
- (d) **Kill's place**: behind more ▾ whatever the state, as on the card; Close is the act when the checklist passes, Kill one menu away for a worktree that must be kept.
- (e) the mockups: regenerated; `FocusReady.dc.html` is the after-wrap-up case.
- (f) the fold control: beside Start, *▸ n sessions* / *▾ n sessions*.
- (g) **two cards named designer-ao-1**: **not reproduced** — no live Org in a cloud session. The client's swap is by id (`old.replaceWith(fresh)`), so two elements need two inserts of one id; the events handler now drops any second element with a card's id after a delta as a guard. **Paul, 2026-09-25: the doubled card is handled under its own TD**, so it leaves this entry's live look.
- Fixed beside it: the header's mode button read *unattended* instead of *Take over* — a card-era line rewrote every mode button's text to the bare mode word after `mode_act` had been set.
- Every side card is a fold remembered per browser; Session starts folded, its summary the profile; the stops control sits on the identity line only while a stop time is set, and in the Session card as *none — set* otherwise.
- **Paul's second round, the same afternoon** (§4.5 *Whose session it is*): the identity line is the **name alone** (no host / repo, the tool's title only when it differs — the designer's read *designer-ao-1* twice); what the session says it is doing left the header for a **Working** card at the top of the side panel, beside Reports (*we already have a Reports card for finished work*); **Close session** and *ready to close ✓* are drawn only on the person's **own** session — interactive, or no team badge — since *I would expect the team members to run themselves and I would be responsible for interactive sessions I started*; a team member's Ready to close is **folded** by default and its note says its team closes it, its Close being the more ▾ entry; and **no team-level close** for the finished ones — the anchor's *Close finished* proposal was declined.

**Reviewed by Sonnet, four rounds in the cloud session (2026-09-25), READY at round 3 and confirmed at round 4.** Taken: the card's Close session gated on *own* like Focus's (round 1); the Forget all row's stale fold label, a member's checklist losing its small Close so more ▾ is the one place (round 2); the stops row as the Session card's control set or not, Forget all into the team header's right-hand cluster with Start and the fold, the first-paint outline following *own* (round 3); the stops row's old sentence on Focus's empty state (round 4). Overruled: "the branch deletes shipped sections" (a two-dot diff against a moved main; main was merged in instead) and "the header's *n ready to close* should count exited sessions" (an exited session's act is Forget, not Close — the row now says *idle*).

**Left for Paul's live look (board line):** the two lines at 1440 and 1280; Close session appearing on a wrapped-up own session without a reload; the fold beside Start; a concluded team's cards in view with *1 ready to close* in its header.

**What happened, as the seed for the review**, 2026-09-25 15:15Z on the designer resumed by Paul (TD-155's record):

1. Paul pressed **Wrap up** on Focus. The session declared *out of work* (`ao progress none`, the wrap-up's words), the record reads `idle` with every Ready-to-close check passing (*tree clean*, *branch pushed*, *no subagents running*, *outcomes reported*). Nothing closes it: a person's Wrap up is only the prompt (§4.5a *Focus: Wrap up*); the policy's `run_until` path is what kills after a wrap-up, and it applies to a stop time, never a press (`_enforce_stop_times`). *Closing is your act; the checklist only says when it is ready* (the side panel's own note). That is the design (§2, goal *only the person closes it*) and this entry does not question it.
2. On **Focus** the header reads *Pop out · Open shell here · VS Code · Copy · Paste · Message · Wrap up · ✕ Kill* — Kill in red as the only stop in sight. **Close** is a small button in the side panel's *Ready to close* card (`focus.html`, `#closebtn`, enabled by `app.js` when every check passes and the state is `idle` or `exited`), below the Reports and Inbox panels, where Paul did not find it. §4.5a says exactly this — *Focus side panel: diff / log / PRs, run-log link, Close* — so the build is faithful and the design is what the review has to change: the card's rule (§4.5 *The card's anatomy*: the next act is the one outlined button in the foot, **Close session** on *ready to close ✓*) has no counterpart on Focus, whose header shows the same controls whatever the session's state.
3. On **Org** the card was there but hidden: the `ao-grind` team had concluded (its manager exited), and a team with nothing live folds its cards behind *n sessions — show* (§4.5a *team groups*). The designer, `idle` and ready to close, counts as nothing live for the fold (`live: 0 if g["stopped"]`, `app.py`), so the header read *concluded 6m ago · out of work · 1 idle · 1 on call · 3 exited · 5 sessions* with a **Start** button, and Paul read it as *already closed*. The folded card carries the caption *ready to close ✓* and the **Close session** button, but the fold hides both, and the header's counts do not say that one of the five is a live session waiting for a press.
