**What the review has to settle:** (a) **Focus after a wrap-up** — when Ready to close passes, does the header carry **Close session** as the outlined next act (the card's rule applied to Focus), with Kill behind *more* or kept as the danger it is; and does the *out of work* badge in the header say *ready to close ✓ · Close* the way the card's slot and caption do; (b) **a concluded team's fold** — a session still there (`idle`, `exited` with a pane) is *live* for the fold, or the fold header carries *1 ready to close* with the Close a press away, or the folded group unfolds itself while it holds anything a person must still close; (c) **what Wrap up promises** — the button's label or its confirm says what happens next (*asks it to finish and push; you close it when the checklist passes*), so a person is not left waiting for a close that is theirs to press; (d) **Kill's place** — whether a `ready to close ✓` session should offer Kill at all, since Close does the same kill after the checks; (e) the mockups (`docs/mockups/gen.py`) for Focus at *ready to close* and for a concluded team holding one; (f) **the fold control's place** (Paul, 2026-09-25): *n sessions — show* sits apart from the team card's other controls, and it took a minute to find — move it beside **Start** so a team card's buttons are together, and give it a show / hide icon (a chevron or an eye) with the count, since the words alone did not read as the way in; (g) **two cards named designer-ao-1** were on the page before Paul closed the resumed designer. The store holds one record and the host agent log reads *designer-ao-1 superseded the exited session of the same name* at the resume (08:06:16 MDT) — TD-145's rule, promoted 2026-09-24 20:16, before the resume — so the doubling was the page's: a card the browser kept after the supersede, or the same id drawn twice by the group and a fold. Reproduce it in the review (resume an exited team member from Focus, watch Org) and say which; the record side is §4.1's rule, one name per team, and held.

**Done when** Paul, having pressed Wrap up on a session, sees on the same screen the one press that closes it once it is ready, and a team card with a session waiting to be closed says so without unfolding — built; his live look closes it; (g) is another TD's.

**Related:** §4.5 *The card's anatomy* (the outlined next act), §4.5a *Focus: Wrap up*, *Focus: Kill*, *Focus side panel: Close*, *card: Close session*, *Org: team groups* (the fold), §4.9a (a declaration is not a state), TD-081 / TD-145 / TD-155 (the resumed session this was seen on), TD-154 (reading a transcript, the other reason the session was resumed at all), TD-095 (the card's foot and slot).

## TD-198: The kind bar's *pickable* bucket swallows design-first entries

**Priority:** Low
**Added:** 2026-09-26 (found with TD-197)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-28 with TD-223 (the designer, PR #723): the entry's (a), taken one step further. The page's kinds are tested *for you*, *design-first*, *pickable*, *other*, so *pickable* means a build a grinder can start; design §4.4 *Repo facts*. Closes with TD-228. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-228.
**Blocked by:** TD-228
**Location:** `src/sessionorc/ledger.py` (`kind_of`: *pickable* is decided before *design-first*), the team card's Repo facet and the Repo page's *Technical debt* lists (`src/agentorc/ui/app.py`), design §4.5a *team card: Repo facet*

**Why:** the page sorts each open entry into exactly one of *pickable, design-first, for you, other*, and *pickable* wins. Since TD-118 put `Pickable: yes` on design-first entries the designer may take, those entries count as *pickable* and never as *design-first*. On 2026-09-26 the bar read *19 pickable · 13 design-first*, and 7 of the 19 were designer-owned design-first entries. A reader takes *pickable* to mean *work a grinder can start*, and the Repo page's pickable list mixes the two.

**Fix:** decide what the buckets mean now that the designer has its own lane: (a) *design-first* wins over *pickable* (Kind before Pickable), so *pickable* means builds; (b) the buckets follow the lanes (*grinder pickable*, *designer pickable*, *for you*, *other*); or (c) pickable becomes a mark within each kind. Then update §4.5a and `kind_of`. TD-197's phase fix does not depend on this.

**Related:** TD-197, TD-118 (the header lines), TD-176 (the facet and the Repo page).

## TD-223: Rule 6 matches nothing in a ledger without Pickable, Owner and Kind lines

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (found walking Paul through dc-grind: *I do not see anything in the inbox from dc-grind*)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-28 (the designer, PR #723, stacked on #721; the steer to Paul is `m-1280433e8140`, bound 2026-09-29 10:24 MDT): design §4.4 *Repo facts* (**Pickable is derived, never written**: the rule, the archive, no header line needed, the page's kinds in order, the written line while it lasts), §6 rule 6 (the lane words over the derived reading), §4.8 *Choosing in a free-pick lane*, §4.5 screen 10's lists, §4.5a *Repo facet*; the glossary's *pickable*. Option (b), agentorc's own reader held equal to the repo's script by a test. Settles TD-198 and answers TD-211. Closes with TD-228. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-228.
**Blocked by:** TD-228
**Location:** design §6 rule 6 (*a lane word matches by the entry's header*), `src/sessionorc/ledger.py` (`lane_matches`, `kind_of`), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (rule 6), the Repo facet's kind bar; dev-cadence's `ledger.py` (pickability derived from `Blocked by:`, its TD-070)

**Why:** rule 6 (TD-195) tells a finished member its lane gained work when an entry matching its lane appears: `free-pick` matches `Pickable: yes` not design-first, `design-first` matches `Kind: design-first` with `Pickable: yes`. dev-cadence's ledger carries none of those lines: its entries have Priority, Type, Status and `Blocked by:`, and its `ledger.py` *derives* pickability from `Blocked by:` (a written `Pickable:` line never overrides, its TD-070). So every dev-cadence entry reads `pickable: ''` in `repos.json`, and rule 6 matches nothing there. On 2026-09-28 dev-cadence's TD-070 (the ledger query script) was pickable by its own tool while grinder-dc-1 sat idle and out of work, its `lane_seen` an empty list. The anchor woke it by hand. The kind bar has the same blind spot: every dev-cadence entry is *other*.

**Fix:** design one reading of pickability for every consumer. Either (a) agentorc reads each repo's ledger through that repo's own `ledger.py` once dev-cadence's TD-070 lands (`--list --json` gives the derived `pickable`, `owner`, `kind`, `type` and the raw header fields), so the header lines are one repo's convention and not the rule's; or (b) agentorc's reader derives pickability from `Blocked by:` as dev-cadence's does when the header has no `Pickable:`. Also decide what a lane word means where there is no `Owner:` (dev-cadence's grinder is the repo's only builder: everything unblocked is its lane). Then rule 6, the kind bar and `ao repo` all read the same way. Done when an entry that becomes pickable in dev-cadence tells an idle, finished grinder-dc-1, and a test covers a ledger with no header lines.

**Paul's question, 2026-09-28:** *should we consider retiring "pickable" and using ledger.py to determine it based on "blocked by" instead?* The anchor's reading: yes, and it is option (a) taken to its end. One rule decides pickability for every consumer, dev-cadence's (derived from `Blocked by:`, its TD-070 `--list --json`), and the written line goes. What the design round has to add: on 2026-09-28 the ledger held 89 `Pickable: no` lines, 17 `yes` and 23 `Blocked by:`. Most of the *no* reasons are not blocks but states: *built, the live check pending* (16 or more), *designed, the build is TD-n* (13), *for evaluation* (5), *the anchor's*, *Paul's*. Each needs a home the derivation reads: a real dependency becomes `Blocked by: TD-n` or `decision (Paul)`; *designed, the build is TD-n* becomes `Blocked by: TD-n` (the design entry closes with its build); *live check* and *evaluation* are already `Kind:` values that no grinder picks; *the anchor's* and *Paul's* are `Owner:`. So the derived rule is *unblocked, Kind build (or design-first for the designer), Owner the reader's role*. That is also what rule 6's lane match, the kind bar (TD-198) and `ao repo` read. `tests/test_ledger.py`'s header test (TD-118) changes with it, and every open entry's `Pickable:` line migrates in one PR with its reason moved, not dropped.

**Related:** TD-195 (rule 6), TD-214 (the wound-down team, the backlog baseline, the missing Owner check), TD-198 (the kind bar's buckets), dev-cadence's TD-070 (the query script) and TD-064 (`Blocked by`).

## TD-228: Build the derived pickable — the reader, the page's kinds, the lane words, the ledger's migration

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** debt
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (the designer, from TD-223's design, with TD-198 and TD-211)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** live-check #904
**Status:** Partly done — **slice 1 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #793):** `sessionorc.ledger` derives `pickable` (`entries(text, archive, resolve, fallback)`, `parse_blocked`, `archive_path`, `Registry` over `Host.repos()`, `entries_before` reading the archive at the same commit), `kind_of`'s new order, `lane_matches` with `build` for no Kind; `tests/test_ledger_derived.py` holds it equal to `scripts/ledger.py --list --json`. No lane gained an entry on this ledger (a bare `free-pick` lost TD-159). **Slice 2 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-2):** a Repo page row says *blocked by …* after its owner (`repo_part.html`, from `blocked_by`), `ao repo` lists in cadence's order, debt before a feature within a priority (`cli._pick_key`), and `ui/org.py`'s comment is corrected; the kind bar, its legend, *n pickable entries wait* and the four lists already read `for_page`, which slice 1 re-derived. No held path. **Slice 3 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #898):** no open entry, the template or the `Fields:` line carries `**Pickable:**` (`tests/test_ledger.py` `test_no_entry_writes_a_pickable_line`; `HEADER` is Owner and Kind), every *no*'s clause moved to its Status as **Next:**, three to a `Blocked by:` pointer instead (TD-108, TD-142, TD-201; TD-189 is blocked by TD-190); ten entries built with a live look left are `Owner: paul`, `Kind: live-check` and three the anchor's (TD-151, whose hover is unbuilt, stays the grinder's, blocked by the designer's word); a Status or a `Blocked by:` set apart below 25 header blocks is back in the block (cadence §2.12); TD-127, TD-158, TD-246 (designed, their builds archived) and TD-211 are archived by it. The two lanes match what was written *yes* before (`[free-pick, owner:grinder]`: TD-228, and TD-255 until #899 built its last slice and blocked it on Paul's look; `[design-first, owner:designer]`: TD-222, TD-262). **What the derived rule changed and the lanes did not:** the page's *pickable* and `ao repo`'s list now hold the anchor's and dev-cadence's unblocked `Kind: build` entries too (TD-004, TD-036, TD-050, TD-080, TD-093, TD-098, TD-114, TD-118, TD-125), since `kind_of` reads no owner; the grinder brief says an entry whose Owner is not the lane's is not the reader's, and `ao repo` prints the owner on each line (`cli.cmd_repo`, built 2026-10-01 by grinder-ao-2: `-` where the header has none; `tests/test_repo_facts.py`), since a run that read ten *pickable* lines found one its own. The briefs: `src/agentorc/briefs/grinder.md`, `entry.md`, `docs/briefs/designer-ao-1.md`; design §4.5 *Add entry* no longer says `Pickable: yes`. **Slice 4 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #901):** `sessionorc.ledger.entries` takes no `fallback` and reads no written line, in the header block or below it (`tests/test_ledger_derived.py` `test_a_written_pickable_line_is_not_read`); design §4.4 *Repo facts*, §4.5 screen 10 and §4.8 *Choosing in a free-pick lane* lose *a written `Pickable: no` still blocks*. **Every slice is built. Left:** the live half of *Done when* — an entry unblocked in dev-cadence's ledger telling an idle, finished grinder-dc-1 with no edit to the entry — after the promote that carries #901; TD-198 and TD-223 archive with this entry then. **Slice 3 waited on nothing (the anchor, 2026-10-01):** `org.yml`'s ao-grind lanes carry `[free-pick, owner:grinder]` and `[design-first, owner:designer]` (the board line closed with #826), and this entry's own written *no* had outlived that wait, which kept the migration out of every lane. **Paul, 2026-10-01, shown that the page counts 8 pickable where `scripts/ledger.py --pickable` derived 84 at that moment (76 on this PR's branch, later the same day):** *lets use the derived pickable - the grinders can tell us if they run into issues* — so slice 3 is the next thing a grinder takes here, slice 4 after it, and an entry the derived rule hands a grinder that it cannot build (a live look, a person's press) is said on the entry, by its `Kind:` or `Owner:`, as slice 3's migration writes them. **From the techlead's read of #793:** fixed before the merge: a pass re-reading one repo resolved `<repo>#TD-NNN` among the re-read repos only, so `_read_repos` now takes the registry's whole list for the `Registry`. The note: `src/agentorc/ui/org.py`'s comment that a pickable design-first entry sits under *pickable* is now false (slice 2's). `entries_before`'s docstring now says a `<repo>#TD-NNN` item resolves at the other repo's tip today.
**Location:** `src/sessionorc/ledger.py` (`FIELD`, `entries`, `kind_of`, `KINDS`, `lane_matches`), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (rule 6 reads the same entries), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao repo`: the `by_kind` line and the lists), `src/agentorc/ui/org.py`, `ui/repo.py` and `ui/templates/team_summary.html` (the kind bar, its legend and *n pickable entries wait*, the Repo page's lists; `org.py`'s comment on *pickable design-first* goes), `tests/test_ledger.py` (`HEADER`, a new equality test) and the tests that write `pickable: "yes"` by hand (`test_lane_news.py`, `test_repo_facts.py`, `test_ui_*`), `docs/technical_debt.md` (the preamble, every open entry), `src/agentorc/briefs/grinder.md`, `docs/briefs/designer-ao-1.md` and any other brief that names the line; design §4.4 *Repo facts*, §6 rule 6. Held paths: the techlead reads slices 1 and 3.

**Why:** TD-223's *Why*: the home reads a written line that dev-cadence's ledger never carries and that cadence §2.4 says is derived, so two tools give two answers and rule 6 is blind in dev-cadence.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **The reader** (`sessionorc`): `entries` reads `**Blocked by:**` and `**Type:**`, takes the archive's ids from `<ledger>_archive.md`, and gives each entry `type`, `blocked_by` and `pickable` by §4.4's rule — an unknown id, an item it cannot read and an item after the decision's comma each keeping the block, the fields read from the header block as the script reads them, an empty line blocking nothing; a `<repo>#TD-NNN` item resolved in the registry's checkout of that repo (`Host.repos()`; the directory's name, or `owner/name` by its origin; `docs/technical_debt.md` and its archive at `origin/<default>`, else the working tree; archived there lifts it; open, unlisted, a name held twice or unfound keeps the block, and `blocked_by` lists the item as written), with a test on two scratch repos and one scratch registry that both readers are pointed at; the archive is the ledger's name with `_archive` before its suffix; a written `Pickable: no` still reads as blocked (the fallback, a parameter of `entries` that the equality test turns off). `kind_of` tests *for you*, *design-first*, *pickable*, *other*. `lane_matches` reads the derived field, with `build` for no Kind. A test holds the set of ids `entries` calls pickable equal to the script's on this repo's ledger, with the fallback off (`python3 scripts/ledger.py --list --json` prints every entry with its derived `pickable`, `type` and `blocked_by`, so the test compares the record and not only the id; `--pickable --json` prints `{pickable, blocked, flags}`), and a second covers a ledger with no header lines: every unblocked entry matches `free-pick`.
2. **The page and `ao repo`**: the lists and the bar from the new kinds; a blocked row says *blocked by TD-n*; `ao repo` lists a lane's pickable entries in cadence's order (Priority, then debt before feature).
3. **The migration and the briefs**, one PR, **not before the lanes carry their owner words** (TD-227 slice 1 built, and `org.yml` edited: the board line of 2026-09-28): every open entry's `**Pickable:**` line goes, its reason moved and never dropped. A dependency becomes `**Blocked by:** TD-n`; *designed, the build is TD-n* becomes `**Blocked by:** TD-n`; a wait on Paul becomes `decision (Paul)` with its pointer; *live check pending*, *for evaluation*, *the anchor's*, *Paul's* are the entry's `Kind:` or `Owner:` where the header already says so, and are **made so where it does not**: on 2026-09-28 about thirty entries were `Kind: build`, unblocked and written *no* — *built; the live look is left* (TD-146, TD-148, TD-152 and others) becomes `Kind: live-check`; *needs an attended run* or *one press in a browser* (TD-005, TD-006, TD-029) becomes `Owner: paul` or `anchor`; *a synced file* (TD-050, TD-061) `Owner: dev-cadence`; a wait on another repo or on a reading (TD-142, TD-201) a `Blocked by:` that names it. The clause moves to the Status where it says more. **The PR's body lists, per lane, the ids matched before and after**: `[free-pick, owner:grinder]` and `[design-first, owner:designer]` match after the migration what was written `yes` before it, every difference named and meant, so rule 6 has no burst to tell. The preamble's paragraph and `tests/test_ledger.py`'s `HEADER` lose the line. The briefs pick from `ao repo`, or from `scripts/ledger.py --pickable` filtered by Owner and Kind, and no brief names the line.
4. **The fallback goes**: `FIELD` stops reading `Pickable`; a ledger that still writes it is read as if it did not.

**Done when:** an entry that becomes unblocked in dev-cadence's ledger tells an idle, finished grinder-dc-1 with no edit to the entry; this ledger has no `**Pickable:**` line and the kind bar counts no design-first entry as pickable; the equality test and the header-less test pass.

**Related:** TD-223, TD-198, TD-211 (each closes with this), TD-227 (rule 8 and the `owner:` lane word, which slice 1 builds on when it is there), TD-118 (the header lines), dev-cadence's TD-070 and TD-064.

## TD-230: The usage chip shows a stale reading with no age, and the gate acts on it as if fresh

**Priority:** High
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (Paul: *our percentage used for the claude sub seems to be stale — our page reads 88%/95% but actual is 93% used*; then: *add how long ago the reading was made in the display*)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Blocked by:** TD-233
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-28 (the designer, PR #734; the steer to Paul is `m-3cb2968e75c3`, bound 2026-09-29 11:24 MDT): design §4.5a **usage** chip (*The age*), §6 *Usage gate* (*A reading the gate can no longer trust*), §5 `usage_gate.max_age`, §4.7 `ao gate`, the Settings page's Usage row. The Fix's three: (1) the age on the chip past five minutes, *unknown since* past three hours; (2) the gate projects from the rate it last saw and pauses on the projection, resuming only on a reading; (3) the Settings card says the age. Closes with TD-233. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-233.
**Location:** design §4.5a **usage** chip, §4.2 (*usage*: keeps the last good reading), §6 *Usage gate*; `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_refresh_usage`), `~/.agentorc/usage.json` (`fetched`), the gate (`ao gate`, `settings.lines`)

**Why:** on 2026-09-28 the chip read *week 88% · stale* while the account was at 93% (Paul, from the account's own page). The held reading's `fetched` was 22:21Z, and every poll since 22:26Z had been refused (TD-231), so by 04:27Z it was six hours old. The chip says *stale* but not how stale, so a person can't tell five minutes from six hours. The gate reads the same reading: with a 5/day week reserve and one day left its line was 95%, so it saw 88% and paused nobody. A stale reading can't show the gate a crossing, and the real account was two points from the line with four agents working and 19 hours to the reset. Paul accepted the risk for the night.

**Fix:** design (1) **the age on the chip**: *week 88% · 6 h ago* (and in `ao gate`'s line), with a threshold past which it reads plainly *unknown since 22:21* (the hover already gives when the reading was taken and why the poll failed, §4.5a; the new part is the age on the chip itself and in `ao gate`); (2) **the gate on a stale reading**: past a set age (e.g. an hour, a setting), the gate no longer treats the held reading as the truth. Either it projects it forward by the rate the account was rising at while readings were fresh (`usage.json` holds only the latest reading per profile, so a short history of readings would need keeping), or it pauses unattended work once the held reading plus a margin reaches the line, or it asks the person with an Inbox row, *usage unknown for 3 h; keep going?*. The round picks, maybe per window. (3) The Settings page's Usage card says the same age. Then the build. Done when a six-hour-old reading says so on the chip, and the gate does not run an account past its line on a reading it can't trust.

**Related:** TD-231 (why the readings stopped), TD-122 (one poll per account; its live check failed on 2026-09-28), TD-087 (the reason on a refused poll), TD-100 (the gate), TD-189 (context bounds; the same *measure, then act* shape).

## TD-231: The usage endpoint answers 429 for hours though agentorc polls once per account

**Priority:** High
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (Paul: *investigate the 429s — we may need to research anthropic's best practices for retrieving usage programmatically*)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Blocked by:** TD-233
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-28 (the designer, PR #734), on the research below and its recommendation: design §4.4 *Usage, reported first and asked for last* and §4.3 `usage_report`. The Fix's step (2), measuring whose calls spend the endpoint's allowance, is not done and is no longer needed to choose: the design stops depending on the endpoint. Closes with TD-233. **Research done 2026-09-28** (the anchor's Sonnet pass, read-only, the endpoint not called; Claude Code 2.1.284). Findings: (1) **no documented way to read a Pro/Max subscription's limits.** The Usage & Cost Admin API needs an admin key, is *unavailable for individual accounts* and reports tokens and cost for API orgs (platform.claude.com/docs/en/manage-claude/usage-cost-api); `/api/oauth/usage` is undocumented. (2) **The statusline gets them, documented**: its stdin JSON carries `rate_limits.five_hour` and `rate_limits.seven_day`, each `{used_percentage, resets_at}`, for Pro/Max only, after the session's first API response, any window possibly absent (code.claude.com/docs/en/statusline). OpenTelemetry has no rate-limit metric; hooks were not found to carry it. (3) **The 429s are widely reported** for Max accounts even at five-minute polling, lasting hours, with `Retry-After` 0 or absent and no Anthropic reply (anthropics/claude-code #30930, #31637 closed *not planned*, #31021). So agentorc's doubling back-off can't learn the window. (4) Model responses carry `anthropic-ratelimit-unified-5h-utilization`, `-7d-utilization` and their resets, named only in the client binary (undocumented). (5) **The installed client calls the same endpoint itself** (`fetchUtilization`, with `?at_wall=1&skip_spend=1` variants; the trigger was not found), so the sessions on the account plausibly share its allowance with agentorc. **The recommendation, for the design round:** stop polling on a timer. The primary source is an agentorc-managed statusline command in each session it launches, set in the adapter's `--settings` layer and chained so a person's own statusline still runs. It writes the `rate_limits` it receives, with a timestamp, to a per-profile file under the home, and the host agent takes the freshest per account and shows its age (TD-230). That is zero extra traffic, fresh whenever any session is active, which is when the gate matters. The endpoint stays only as a fallback on demand: at most once per 15 minutes per account, a fixed hour's cool-off after a 429 and nothing during it. Caveats: an idle account gives a stale reading (TD-230's rule covers it), and the statusline fields need a client version that has them (v2.1.80 and later, per #31637), a window is dropped once its `resets_at` passes, and nothing is there before a session's first API response. The per-token-not-per-account reading of the limit in #30930 and #31637 is community speculation. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-233.
**Location:** `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/__init__.py` (`USAGE_URL = https://api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage`, the `anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20` header, `usage_for`, `UsageRefused`, `_retry_after`), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_refresh_usage`: one poll per account, `USAGE_EVERY` 300 s, the doubling back-off to `USAGE_BACKOFF_MAX` 3600 s), design §4.2 (*usage*)

**Why:** TD-122 (built 2026-09-23, #517) cut the polls to one per account, because four profiles on one login had been refused. The refusals are back. The agent's log on 2026-09-28 reads *rate_limited (was ok)* at 16:26 MDT and *rate_limited (was no reading yet)* at 21:26 after a restart, with the last good reading at 22:21Z. The endpoint then refused every poll for six hours or more. agentorc asks at most every five minutes, less once backed off. So either the endpoint's allowance is far tighter than that, or something else on the account spends it: every Claude Code client may read the same endpoint for its own `/usage` and status, and on this account a dozen sessions run at once. The endpoint is not a documented API: agentorc reads it as Claude Code does, with the user's OAuth token and a beta header.

**Fix:** (1) **research** (Sonnet, read-only, with sources): whether Anthropic documents a supported way to read a Pro/Max subscription's usage programmatically (and if not, what the supported surfaces are: the Claude Code `/usage` command, the statusline's input JSON if it carries rate-limit fields, response headers such as `anthropic-ratelimit-*` on the model calls a session already makes); the endpoint's limits if stated anywhere; what the Claude Code client polls and how often; whether a `Retry-After` is sent (the agent's log records only the reason's transitions, so this needs the headers logged). (2) Measure here: count this account's calls to the endpoint across the sessions (is it us, or them?) and log the response headers of a refusal. (3) Then choose, in the design round the research makes needed: read usage from a supported surface (e.g. the headers or statusline data the sessions already receive, reported through the hook), poll less and share the reading, or stop polling while any session can report it. Done when the chip holds a reading under an hour old through a busy evening, and the source is one Anthropic supports or tolerates.

**Related:** TD-230 (the age on the chip, the gate on a stale reading), TD-122 (one poll per account), TD-087 (the reason), TD-073 (the reading), design §4.2, §4.2a.

## TD-233: Build usage reported first, its age, and the gate's projection

**Priority:** High
**Type:** debt
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (the designer, from TD-231's and TD-230's designs)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** live-check #791
**Status:** Built, live check in part — **2026-10-04: Paul confirmed the chip's *week 68%* against the account's usage page**, and `ao gate` reads *read 5s ago (reported)*, one reading per account (TD-122 closed on it). **Left:** a reading that goes old saying its age on the chip and in `ao gate`, and the gate pausing on a projection — live events, the anchor's when one happens. **slice 1 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-2):** the age on the chip (`usage_chip` / `AO.usageChip`, `USAGE_AGED`, `USAGE_FRESH`, `USAGE_UNKNOWN` in `ui/common.py` and `app.js`; the chip drawn again each minute from `data-usage`), `ao gate`'s *· read 6h ago (asked)* (`_gate_read`, from the `usage` RPC beside `gate`), and the Settings card's age line (`settings_page.reading_age`); *stale* gone. No held path. **Slice 3 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #768):** `_refresh_usage` asks an account only when its reading is older than `USAGE_FRESH` (900 s, `_usage_young`) and at most once per `USAGE_FRESH`; a 429 cools it off `USAGE_COOL` (an hour), a longer `Retry-After` kept, and its end (`cool_until` on the reading) held across a restart; the doubling, `USAGE_EVERY` and `USAGE_BACKOFF_MAX` went. **A window past its reset pauses nothing, built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #776, ahead of slice 4, at the anchor's ask):** `settings.lines` marks the row `unknown: "reset"` and `crossed` skips it. **Slice 2's host-agent half built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #770):** the `usage_report` RPC (`agent_wake.py`, the caller the session), `sessionorc.usage` (`merge`: reports by the highest inside one reset, the endpoint's answer outright, resets within `RESET_SLACK` one window; `asked_only_due`, the hourly ask for an endpoint-only window, `USAGE_ONLY_EVERY`), each window's `at`, `source` and `history` (one point per ten minutes, three hours). **Slice 2's adapter half built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #780):** `agentorc-hook --statusline` (`hook.statusline`: `report_due`, a report on a change or once a minute, `fresh` from `cost.total_api_duration_ms`, the last sent in `statusline/<session>.json` under the home, one second's timeout, nothing queued; then the displaced line with the same stdin), the adapter's `usage_report`, `displaced_status_line` and the layer's `statusLine` (`STATUSLINE_REFRESH`, the displaced `padding` in a `+pad<n>` layer); §4.4 *The poll, as built* says *while nothing reports* (the techlead's read of #770). **A node's report to the home built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #783):** the link method `usage_report {id, account, windows, fresh}` (`_forward_usage_report`, `_take_usage_report`), the node's account key remembered per node and profile (`_usage_remote_keys`), the home keeping and showing it (`_usage_remote_live`) and never asking for it. **The home's send built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #786):** `usage_reading {account, reading}` home → node (`_push_usage_readings`, `_usage_told`; the node's `_take_usage_reading` and `usage.adopt`, window by window), with the techlead's two notes on #783: a node's report under a profile metered at the home is taken, and a node's account is seeded from its profile's held reading after a home restart (the passes before its first report keep it). Slice 2 is done. **From the techlead's read of #786, for when the entry next moves:** (1) a node's endpoint answer never reaches the home (only reports are forwarded), so for an account only the node asks for, a number the endpoint lowered at the node is overwritten by the home's higher, report-merged number at the next fresh report — it errs toward pausing early; say so in §4.4 or forward the node's answer; (2) `adopt` compares `at` alone inside one window, so a number the home raised from a report that was not fresh is sent and not taken — rare, and it errs the other way; (3) `_push_usage_readings` sits on the reply path of a home session's `usage_report` RPC, so a wedged link holds each reply up to `REPORT_WRITE` per node against the status line's one-second timeout — detach it, as the node's forward is. **Slice 4's host-agent half built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #781):** `usage.max_age` (`settings.parse_max_age`, `max_age`, `usage`; `set_settings usage=`), the one reader `usage.project` (`rate`, `RATE_SPAN`, `RATE_MIN`) behind `_gate_windows` for the gate, `_profile_gated` and `gate`, the mark's `projected`, a pause held while its window is unknown for want of a rate, and the two notes (`_projection_notes`). **Slice 4's client half built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #789):** `ao gate --max-age <age|off|default>` (`_max_age_said`), the projected window's print (`now 88% · … · projected 96%`, *· no rate to project by*), the Settings page's **trust a reading for** (one field above the accounts: `usage.max_age` is one key; `POST /api/settings/max_age`) and a reserve's line carrying the projection. **The chip's *projected* built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #791):** `usage_chip` and `AO.usageChip` read the gate's row (`_projected` / `projectedOf`) — *week 88% · 6h · projected 96% / 95%*, the worst window and the colour from the projection, a projected reading never *unknown since*. Slice 5's tests are in with slices 2–4. **Left:** the live half of *Done when* (the board line, due 2026-10-03), then archive this entry with TD-230 and TD-231 **Next:** built; what is left is the live check.
**Location:** `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/__init__.py` (`hooks_settings`: the `statusLine` key; `usage_report`; `usage_for`, `UsageRefused`), the hook command (`src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/hook.py`, `agentorc-hook --statusline`, built), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_refresh_usage`, `_profile_gated`, the gate's pass, `USAGE_FRESH` and `USAGE_COOL`, which `agent.py` imports), `src/sessionorc/agent_wake.py` (a `usage_report` RPC beside `rpc_hook`; `set_settings`, `_reserves_change`), `usage_store` and `usage.json`, `src/sessionorc/settings.py` (a fifth key, `usage.max_age`), `src/sessionorc/agent_link.py` and `agent_remote.py` (the report, node to home, and the reading, home to node, built), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao gate`), `src/agentorc/ui/` (the chip, the Settings page's Usage card), `tests/`; design §4.4 *Usage*, §4.3, §4.5a **usage** chip, §6 *Usage gate*. Held path: the techlead reads the slices in `src/sessionorc/**`.

**Why:** TD-230's and TD-231's: a six-hour-old reading ran the account to within two points of its line unseen, because the one source was an endpoint that refuses for hours.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **The age, first, since it needs no new source**: every reading's `at` reaches the chip and `ao gate`; the chip prints the age past five minutes, dims past `USAGE_FRESH`, reads *unknown since … (was n%)* past `USAGE_UNKNOWN`; the word *stale* goes from the chip (`ui/help.py` does not name it); the Settings page's Usage card shows age and source.
2. **The report**: `hooks_settings` sets `statusLine` to `agentorc-hook --statusline` with a refresh of sixty seconds; the command reads stdin, takes `rate_limits` through the adapter's `usage_report` (the percentage a float, the reset epoch seconds; `fresh` from the payload's running totals of API work, which the build names after reading a real payload), sends it as the `usage_report` RPC when a number or a reset changed or sixty seconds passed (a small file per session under the home holds the last sent), with a timeout of a second and no queue, then runs the status line it displaced (local, project, then profile settings; its padding kept) with the same stdin and prints its output, nothing when there is none; it never fails the status line: any error is swallowed and the chained command still runs. The host agent merges per account and window (the highest inside one `resets`, a later `resets` outright, `at` moved only by a fresh report or an answer) and keeps the newest reading and one per ten minutes for three hours; a node sends reports on to the home by a link method of its own, dropped when the link is down. A payload with no `rate_limits` (an old client, a metered profile, before the first response) reports nothing.
3. **The endpoint as the fallback**: `_refresh_usage` asks only for an account with a live session and no reading younger than `USAGE_FRESH`, at most once per `USAGE_FRESH`, and not for `USAGE_COOL` after `rate_limited`; the doubling back-off goes; it is asked by the host holding the profile's credentials; a window only the endpoint gives is asked for once an hour while a reserve names it or it stood within ten points of its cap; a window past its `resets` reads unknown and is never a cap for `limited`.
4. **The projection**: `usage.max_age` (`set_settings`, `ao gate --max-age`, the Settings field; `off` allowed); **one reader** gives a reading or a projection to the gate's pass and to `_profile_gated` alike; past `max_age`, for a profile with unattended sessions live and a window whose `resets` is ahead, it reads held + rate × age, at most 100, the rate between the oldest and newest kept readings inside two hours, never below zero and none under a span of twenty minutes; the gate pauses on a projection that reaches the line (a team's reserve priority counted), the mark and one `system` note saying so; an unknown window or no rate pauses nothing and notes *usage unknown*, once per account per day, never under `off`; a pause on a projection ends on a reading under the line, on the window's reset, or on `max_age` or a reserve moved.
5. **Tests**: a reading of 88% taken six hours ago with a rate of one point an hour and a line of 95% does not pause, and with two points an hour does; no history, or one spanning ten minutes, pauses nothing and files one note; a fresh reading under the line resumes and a projection never does; a pause on a projection lifts when the window's reset passes; a profile paused on a projection is not restarted by the keep-running pass; a report that is not fresh moves no age; two sessions reporting 61.5 and 60.0 inside one reset read 61.5; a project's own status line is the one chained when the profile names another; the status-line command with a person's own command prints that command's output and reports once for ten redraws inside a minute; a payload without `rate_limits` reports nothing and breaks nothing; after a 429 the endpoint is not asked for an hour; a report from a node reaches the home's chip; a window past its reset reads unknown.

**Done when:** through a busy evening the chip's reading is under fifteen minutes old with the endpoint asked at most a handful of times, a reading that does go old says its age on the chip and in `ao gate`, and the gate pauses the team on a projection rather than run the account past its line.

**Related:** TD-230, TD-231 (the designs and the research), TD-122 (one poll per account), TD-087 (the reason on a refusal), TD-100 (the gate), TD-201 (the unattended layer's settings).

## TD-247: The manager's remaining jobs are mostly mechanical: move them to the tick, and make its judgement a seat on call

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** debt
**Added:** 2026-09-30 (Paul: *if the manager is required for a team (wind downs, etc) we should probably default every team to having one. Ideally we would not need to and all of its required jobs could be scripted or given to the agent host, but let's investigate*; the anchor's read with two Sonnet readers, of the brief, design §6 and the code, nothing run)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-30 (the designer, PR #845; the steer to Paul on when the default flips is `m-7b65cae16909`, bound 2026-10-01 10:06 MDT): design §6 *What is left is judgement, and a seat holds it*, rules 10–12, rule 3 *A manager on call is a seat of this rule*, rule 4's owed clause and rule 6's dropped lease; §4.8 *session, not code*; §4.9 `manager.on_call`; §4.9b the `team` trigger; §4.5a the rows **cadence check failed**, **merged without its read**, **idle · open work** and the seat slot's words; the glossary. The Fix as written, with the round's decisions: the four jobs are three rules and a clause of rule 4, none typed by a session; the judgement is the `manager` role as a seat with a trigger of its own (the techlead holds no grant and stays the ladder); no permission allow-list, since an unattended launch skips permissions already; `control` stays on the seat's record across the close; on the default the designer steered *a standing manager until dc-grind has run on call once* and Paul chose the alternative (2026-10-01, answer 2 of `m-7b65cae16909`): on call is the default from the build, every team at its next Start; dc-grind's first run is TD-259's last slice as a first look, not a gate. Closes with TD-258 and TD-259. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-258 (the policies) and TD-259 (the seat).
**Blocked by:** TD-258, TD-259
**Location:** design §6 (*Keeping a team running*, *What stays the manager's*), §4.8 (*The manager is a session, not code*, *Waking a manager*, `manager: {role: person}`), §4.9a, §4.9b (the seat rule); `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md` (*A round*, *Out of work*, *Escalate*), `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md`, `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py`, `src/agentorc/org.py` (the default manager), `scripts/check_cadence.py`, `scripts/cadence_changes.py`

**Why:** what the read found. (1) **A manager is already every team's default and is not required**: a definition with no `manager:` gets one named `<team>-lead` (`org.py`), `manager: {role: person}` starts none, and supervision keys on no role or controller, so rules 1–7 and the balance rule act on a person-led team's members as on any. No team on kmaster is defined that way (four teams, four managers), so the shape is unproven. (2) **The one thing a person-led team cannot do is end**: nothing closes members that have declared and sit idle, so the team never reads wound down and rule 8 never starts it again; rule 9 does that and is TD-241, raised to High with this entry (Paul, 2026-09-30) and partly built. With a manager the same step hangs on its memory (TD-240), which is what left ao-grind with no Start that day. (3) **Of what the brief still gives the manager, four jobs read fields and scripts, not screens**: the cadence check on a member's PR (`check_cadence.py --pr N --json` gives a verdict per row), the held-path check (a PR merged on `src/sessionorc/**` or `docs/briefs/**` with no read from the seat), the relay of `docs/cadence-changes.md` entries once per member (`cadence_changes.py --json`, today `manager-ao-1.relayed.json`), and the chase of an owed outcome (rule 4 already names an entry the person handed). (4) **What needs a reader is occasional**: an `ask` addressed to the manager, a permission or a question outside an allow-list, a `stalled?` or *idle · open work* member read from its tail, the second reading of the ledger before a wind-down, and the prose of an escalation. For that a manager wakes every 25 to 60 minutes and mostly finds nothing: manager-ao-1 stood at 686k of context on 2026-09-30, its rounds reading *nothing needs a person*.

**Fix — a design round, in this order:**
1. **The four mechanical jobs as tick policies**, each a fixed line and an Inbox row on the second failure, none typed by a session: the cadence check when a member reports `done` with a `pr` or its PR merges; the held-path check on a merge; the relay per member per entry; the outcome chase as rule 4's own. The round says which run only on the home, what a node's member gets, and what the `review` row's *self-attested* means when no session reads the PR.
2. **The judgement that is left, as a seat on call** (§4.9b's shape, as the techlead is): filled by a trigger the tick computes — a member `stalled?` or *idle · open work* past its nudge, a pending permission or question on an unattended member, an `ask` addressed to the team's manager — and closed when it has answered. The round says whether that is the `manager` role with a trigger or the techlead seat with a wider brief, what holds `control` while no manager is live (a seat's grant, as now, or nothing), and whether a permission allow-list in the definition takes the common prompts off it.
3. **The default.** Whether a definition with no `manager:` should then mean the seat rather than a standing session, and what `manager: {role: person}` means beside it; and rule 3's fill ceiling, which groups seats by a shared controller and so gives each seat of a person-led team its own.
4. **A first look, not a gate** (as asked: *proof before the default moves*; Paul, 2026-10-01, chose to flip the default at the build): dc-grind's first run on call, whose needs are the most mechanical, watched, and what the seat could not do written in TD-259.

**Done when:** the design says, for each line of the manager brief's round, which tick policy or which seat trigger has it; TD-103's *done when* (a night with the brief silent) holds because the brief no longer lists those jobs; and a build entry carries the slices.

**Related:** TD-103 (the first move of policy to the tick: built, live check pending), TD-240 and TD-241 (finished as the home's reading), TD-214 and TD-227 (rule 8), TD-239 (balance), TD-245 and TD-246 (the early restart, the person's restart), TD-098 (seats with triggers), TD-118 (one model for every role, the round's cost).

No live look yet: no team is on call until the flip, so the look is slice 6's. **Slice 2 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #878), without the flip:** `_idle_open` writes `idle_open: {at, ref}` (a member's alone; `ref` none for an owed outcome alone); `_team_causes` and `_seat_due` set `seat_due: {at, by, member}` for `trigger: team` — `ask`, the question's id, in `member`'s place for `asks`; `seat_filled` is written on the record that took the seat (`_fill`) and loses an entry when its cause goes; an idle seat with a member's reading due is closed and filled for it (`_seat_done`; design §6 rule 3 says so); `Session.asks_ids`. Tests in `tests/test_manager_on_call.py`, the ceiling's groups among them. **Slice 3 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #879):** `_sweep_mail` leaves an `ask` to a closed record carrying `seat` open and marks it pending, as an exited addressee's is (its bound and Forget still expire it); `work.manager_of`, `work.finished`, rule 9 and rule 1 needed no change for a closed manager on call, and `tests/test_seat_closed.py` holds each — *the seat is not filled for a finished team* is held there for a seat with nothing due, not as a reading of the `team` trigger. **The flip built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, in grinder-ao-2's package while it is out of work, PR #884):** `org.ON_CALL_DEFAULT` is `True` (its test in `tests/test_org.py`; three tests that stood a declaring manager now stand a member, and a team start's manager carries `seat: {trigger: team}`); the default is the `manager` role's alone (another role managing has no seat's template: `on_call: true` is honoured, and its brief is then its own to write); `teamrun._seat_of` reads a manager as a seat from its record's `seat`, so a running standing manager is not drawn as an empty seat; `cards.py` reads *idle · open work* from `idle_open` alone; `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` is written for a seat and the primer's decided table has the row. **Nothing changes on a running team**: ao-grind's manager stays standing until the promote and the team's next Start. Slice 6 open — the first look on dc-grind, after the promote; its line is on the board. **From the techlead's reads of #875 and #878, settled in #878:** design §4.9's `on_call` key says the slot and the trigger are built and that the default waits on slice 3 and the flip; §4.4a's `idle_open`, `seat_filled` read *built*. **A gap from the read of #878, for whoever puts a manager on call on a node:** `_fill` reads the new record from `self.remote` right after `_route_act("create")`, before the node has pushed it, so a node-hosted manager on call gets no `seat_filled` entry and the same standing cause fills it again, under the ceiling — `_replay` has the same shape for `restarts`. **The wording gap from the techlead's read of #884, settled 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #886):** §4.8's role paragraph, §6 rule 3 and §6 *What is left is judgement* now say the default is the `manager` role's alone, as §4.9 does. **Next:** slices 1–5 and the flip are built; what is left is slice 6, the first look on dc-grind after a promote.
**Location:** `src/agentorc/org.py` (`ManagerDef`, `MANAGER_KEYS`: `on_call`; refused beside `role: person`), `src/agentorc/teams.py` (`Launch.create_params`: `seat: {trigger: team}` on the manager; `_trigger`), `src/agentorc/teamrun.py` (the manager created first, as now), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_seat_due`: the `team` trigger's four readings and `seat_due.member`; `seat_filled`; `idle_open` written beside `nudged_at`; `_fill`'s groups unchanged and tested), `src/sessionorc/agent_inbox.py` (`_sweep_mail`: an `ask` to a closed record carrying `seat` is spared), `src/sessionorc/work.py` (`manager_of` finds a manager that carries `seat`; `finished` reads a closed manager on call as not live), `src/agentorc/ui/cards.py` (*idle · open work* from `idle_open`; the slot's words), `src/agentorc/ui/inbox.py` (the **idle · open work** row for a team with no manager), `src/agentorc/ui/help.py` and §4.5a's help list, `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao team list`: *manager: on call*), `src/agentorc/briefs/manager_on_call.md` (the seat's template, beside `manager.md`; built), `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` and `docs/briefs/techlead-context.md` (held), `tests/`

**Why:** TD-247's *Why* (4): what still needs a reader is occasional, and a standing manager spends a round an hour finding nothing. Design §6 rule 3 *A manager on call is a seat of this rule*, *What is left is judgement*, §4.9 `on_call`, §4.9b the `team` trigger, §4.5a the slot and the row, §4.8 *session, not code* say the shape; this entry builds it. `src/sessionorc/**` and `docs/briefs/**` are held paths: those slices wait for the techlead's read.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **The definition and the launch.** `on_call: true|false` on `manager:` (default `true` — Paul, 2026-10-01; an unknown value, `on_call` beside `role: person`, or `on_call` on a member — `MEMBER_KEYS` is built from `MANAGER_KEYS` — is an error naming it); `ao team start` writes `seat: {trigger: team}` on the manager's record when true, `supervised` as now, `control` as now; `ao team list` and `--json` say it. Tests: the key read, refused where the design says, the record's `seat` written.
2. **The trigger.** `_seat_due` for `trigger: team`: `by` one of `asks` (as the techlead's), `pending` (a controlled supervised member hook-confirmed `needs-you` with `pending.kind == permission`), `stalled` (a controlled member `stalled?`), `open` (a controlled member carrying `idle_open`); `seat_due: {at, by, member}`; `seat_filled: [{member, by, at}]` on the seat's record, an entry dropped when its cause is gone, a standing cause raising no second `seat_due`. `idle_open: {at, ref}` written by the tick on any supervised member still hook-confirmed idle `IDLE_NUDGE` after `nudged_at` with the same work open, cleared on a state change; `cards.py` reads the slot from it. The fill ceiling's grouping gets a test for a person-led team (each seat its own group) and a managed one (the techlead and auditors together, the manager seat alone). Tests: each `by` fills once; the same standing cause does not refill; a question or menu fills nothing; the seat is closed idle with no `seat_due` after `SEAT_IDLE_GRACE`.
3. **The sweep and the readings.** `_sweep_mail` leaves an `ask` to a closed record carrying `seat` open for the fill, for every seat; `work.manager_of` already returns a record that holds `control` and is listed in `controllers` whether or not it carries `seat`, and `work.finished` already passes over a dead manager, so a closed manager on call leaves the team's reading to hold without it and rule 9 closes the members and writes the note — tests for both, no change; rule 9 sends no line to a closed manager and the seat is not filled for a finished team; rule 1 leaves the manager seat to rule 3 as any seat. Tests: a question to a closed seat survives a tick whose fill was refused; a team whose manager on call is closed is finished when its members have declared; `manager did not close` is never raised for a closed manager on call.
4. **The person-led row and the slot.** The **idle · open work** Inbox row for a member of a team with no manager (`manager_of` empty), Open and Snooze, from `idle_open`; the seat slot's *on call — comes when a member needs a reading*; the help paragraphs together with §4.5a's help list (wording this slice's). Tests: the row drawn for a person-led team's member and not for a managed one; the slot's words.
5. **The brief.** `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md` for a seat on call: read `seat_due.by` and `member` from your own record (`ao status --json` on yourself), read the inbox and `ao team status --json`, act on the one reading named — answer the `ask`; allow or deny the permission by the brief's rule; `ao explain` and one send for a `stalled?` member; `ao tail` and one send or a `steer` to the person for *idle · open work* — log one line with `ao log`, and `/exit` as the techlead does; no round, no `ao wait`, no board lines (a `steer` or an `ask` to the person, §4.10), no declaration (a seat is empty or filled). One template that branches on its own record's `seat`, or two files, is the build's call; `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` follows, and `docs/briefs/techlead-context.md`'s decided table gains the row *what is the tick's and what is the manager's; a manager on call is the default from the build (Paul, 2026-10-01)* → §6, §4.9, TD-247 (held).
6. **The first look** (TD-247 step 4, as Paul decided it on 2026-10-01: the default flips at the build, so this is a look and not a gate). Once slices 1–5 are live and promoted, every team is on call at its next Start; the first run of dc-grind on call — whose needs are the most mechanical — is watched: fills by `by`, what each cost, what the seat could not do, written here under **First look:** with a ledger entry for anything it could not do; then this entry archives with TD-247. Nothing in `org.yml` is a session's to edit: a team that should stay standing is Paul's `on_call: false` line.

**Done when:** on a scratch home, a team defined with `on_call: true` starts a manager that finds nothing and exits; a `steer` to it, a member's permission prompt, a member `stalled?` and a member *idle · open work* each fill it once and it closes after acting; the same standing cause does not refill it; its members' `controllers` name it across the close; a question sent to it while closed is there at the fill; the team is wound down by rule 9 with the manager closed; dc-grind's first run on call is written here.

**Related:** TD-247 (the design), TD-103 (rule 3, the seat rule), TD-098 (seats with a trigger), TD-240 / TD-241 (rule 9), TD-256 (rule 9's marks on a resumed manager), TD-245 / TD-249 (the manager outside the context bound), TD-250 (mail on a replay), TD-258 (the policies), TD-118 (one model for every role — a seat's fills are the round's cost made visible).

## TD-290: A UI change goes to the person unchecked — the grinder verifies it, a reviewer only if needed, and what is left is a `look`

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-10-02 (the anchor, from Paul's walk of the pages: *we should probably have these types of verification items sent to a reviewer (and then to me if needed)*; 2026-10-03: *I would expect a grinder who makes ui changes to verify them itself before sending to a reviewer, and only send to a reviewer if needed*; *a distinction between "watch" items (issues that have arisen once and need to be seen again before acting) and "look" items (items that are for me to take a look at) — have the look items go to either steering or "needs you" instead of creating a new category*)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** **Designed 2026-10-03**, twice (the designer). In the morning, on Paul's three points (PR #947; the two choices steered as `m-71d3c709e116`, Paul: *Go with both defaults: the techlead seat is the reviewer … and a look the techlead leaned Works on closes as Works at its Due date*): what was left for the person was a board `look` line, drawn under Steering with a default and closed by its team at its `Due:` — **superseded the same evening**. In the evening, on Paul's note `m-d435ff0d831a` (PR #977): a look that reaches the person is mail. Design as it stands: §4.9b *A UI change is verified by its builder* (the builder's scratch home and the **UI check** in its PR; the live copy read by a `watch`, never pressed; the reviewer is the techlead seat, its three replies; what is left is nothing, a `steer` with the default *Works* where the techlead leaned, or an `ask` with *Works* / *Not right* where nobody could settle it; a board `look` only without agentorc's mail), §4.10 *A look* (`shots`), §4.7 `--shot`, §4.5 screen 6, §4.5a **Inbox row: a look** and its **Send to reviewer** (`inbox_hand`), the glossary's *look* and *scratch home*, mockup `Inbox.dc.html` (`docs/mockups/reviews/2026-10-03-td290-look-rows.png`). Nothing is asked of dev-cadence. Builds: TD-291 (the builder's half; built and archived), TD-292 (the mail's and the Inbox's half).
**Blocked by:** TD-292
**Location:** design §4.5a (a board row's answers, **Works** / **Not right…**; the Inbox's sections), §4.9 (roles), `docs/briefs/grinder-ao-*.md` (*never run `ao ui`*; *a fix that needs a live look is … a `watch` line*), cadence §3.5 (the live look, written as a `watch`); the anchor's Playwright set-up (`~/ao-shots/pwlib`, `~/ao-shots/scripts/`, headless Chromium)

**Why:** today no one checks a UI change before the person does. A grinder may not run `ao ui` (its brief), and the live copy changes only at the anchor's promote, so every merged UI change ends in a `watch` line asking the person to look, and most of what those lines ask is mechanical: a label reads so, a button is greyed, a toast says *x*, a size takes. The board held ten at once on 2026-10-02 and Paul walked them page by page over an evening. A headless browser can make most of those checks: Playwright with Chromium loads a page, waits for its scripts and the terminal, reads values and takes screenshots; it settled TD-288's fresh-load half. And the word is wrong: `watch` means a thing seen once that must be seen again before acting (a session's §6 check), while these lines ask the person to look.

**Fix:** design first. *(Point (3) below is the entry as it was raised; Paul's note of 2026-10-03 evening replaced it — what is left for the person is mail, a `steer` or an `ask`, and never a board line where agentorc's mail reaches: see Status.)* (1) **The grinder verifies its own UI change.** Before the PR, against a scratch UI: a temporary `AGENTORC_HOME` and a private port, the same kind of thing as `pdm run test`, never the live units, so the brief's rule changes from *never run `ao ui`* to *never run it against the live home*. It drives the change with Playwright and puts the screenshots and the values it read in the PR. After the promote, it makes a read-only look at the live copy. (2) **A reviewer only if needed:** for what the grinder cannot settle by itself, such as a judgement of layout or wording, or a state it cannot stage. Say who that reviewer is (the techlead seat, or a reviewer role with a browser) and what it sends on. (3) **What is left for the person is a `look` item, not a `watch`** (dev-cadence TD-082 adds the kind). It carries the screenshots, says what was checked and what is the person's to judge, and draws in **Needs you**, or in **Steering** when it carries a default and a bound. It does not get an Inbox section of its own. `watch` keeps its meaning. (4) A **Send to reviewer** answer on a `look` row, for when the person would rather have it checked first, and what that writes on the board. Keep it one rule: a look that a machine can settle never waits on the person.

**Done when:** the design says who verifies a UI change and how the rest reaches the person, the grinder briefs say it, the build entries are on the ledger with a `Blocked by:` line, and the next merged UI change goes through it.

**Related:** TD-255 (a live look's answers), TD-244 (the page walks), TD-288 (settled half by a headless look), cadence §3.5.

## TD-292: Build the mail half of a look

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-10-03 (the designer, TD-290's build; re-scoped the same evening on Paul's note: a look is mail)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** live-check #1007
**Status:** **Built; a live check waiting on an event (grinder-ao-2, 2026-10-05).** #1007, the last slice, is live (`ao promote status`: live f8090c2). Read that day with a headless GET of the live Inbox: no look mail is on it — no row draws a `/shot/` screenshot and none carries a seat's reading — so none of the *Done when*'s three readings can be made yet. They wait on the first look a builder sends after a page PR (design §4.9b), and Send to reviewer's on Paul pressing it on one. Nobody can cause that on purpose, so the entry is the anchor's until a look has arrived; whoever reads one then writes what it showed here. Before: the board row's pair on cadence's kind `look` is built: PR #975 (`LOOK_KINDS`), slice 1 of the entry as it was first scoped. Re-scoped 2026-10-03 evening (PR #977) to the mail rows. Slice 1 built — PR #980 (the grinder brief's three cases as mail; the techlead preset's two sentences). Slice 2 built — PR #983 (`ao msg --shot`; `shots` on the envelope, checked whole against `docs/mockups/reviews/<name>.png`). Slice 3, the row, built — PR #982 (`look_shots`, `look_pair`, `GET /repo/<repo>/shot/<name>.png`; this host's registry only, another host's is TD-300). Slice 4a, the host agent's half of Send to reviewer, built — PR #990 (`inbox_hand`, `snoozed_for`, `look`, `looked_by`; the techlead preset's *A look handed to you*). Slice 4b built — PR #1007 (the page's **Send to reviewer**, the seat read from `org.yml`; the snoozed row's *with <seat> since <time>*; the handed `ask` read as a look; the `looked_by` line above the answers). Merged, live look pending: the *Done when*'s Send to reviewer, with a real techlead seat.
**Location:** `docs/briefs/grinder-ao-1.md` (the paragraph *A change to a page is verified by you*, the one brief that carries it), `src/agentorc/briefs/techlead.md` (*A screenshot ask*: *The builder then puts a board line before the person*; *what reaches the person is the builder's board line, one road*), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao msg --shot`), `src/sessionorc/mail.py` and `src/sessionorc/models.py` (`shots`, `snoozed_for`, `read_by` on an envelope), `src/sessionorc/agent_inbox.py` (`inbox_hand`), `src/agentorc/ui/inbox.py` and `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` (the row, the screenshot route); design §4.9b *A UI change is verified by its builder*, §4.10 *A look*, §4.7, §4.5a **Inbox row: a look** and **Send to reviewer**; mockup `docs/mockups/Inbox.dc.html`, `docs/mockups/reviews/2026-10-03-td290-look-rows.png`

**Why:** Paul, 2026-10-03 (his note `m-d435ff0d831a`): *rework TD-290 so a look that reaches the person is mail, not a board line*; his reason there: *a steer already means go with the default at the bound unless I say otherwise, which is what the leaned-Works look wants*. The briefs TD-291 landed (#972, #973) still tell a builder to write a board line.

**Fix:** as §4.9b's table and §4.5a's two rows say, in slices, each its own PR. (1) **The briefs** (touches `docs/briefs/**`: the reader's read; needs nothing built): this repo's grinder brief says the three cases as mail, and the techlead preset's two sentences name the builder's message in place of its board line — nothing when every check held; `ao msg person --kind steer --default "Works" --bound <no shorter than a night> --about TD-NNN "…"` naming the techlead's reply and the screenshots' paths where it leaned; `--kind ask --answer "Works" --answer "Not right: <what>"` where nobody could settle it — sent after the merge, the first paragraph the one thing that is Paul's to judge; a *Not right* reply is ledgered as an entry of its own and its outcome reported; no board line, but the `watch` for a read of the live copy stays. (2) **`--shot`** (touches `src/sessionorc/**`: the reader's read): up to four, a `.png` under `docs/mockups/reviews/` of the sender's repo, only on a `steer` or an `ask` to the person, refused otherwise in words; `shots` on the envelope; the briefs of slice 1 then name the screenshots with it. (3) **The row**: `GET /repo/<repo>/shot/<name>.png` serving that one directory at `origin/<default>` and nothing else; thumbnails under a look's text, on the `steer` and the `ask` row alike; on the `ask`, the pair *Works* / *Not right: <what>* drawn as **Works** and **Not right…**. (4) **Send to reviewer** (touches `src/sessionorc/**`): `inbox_hand {id}`, the person's alone — the `handed` `ask` to the sender's team's techlead seat with the look's text, `shots` and id; the look snoozed with `snoozed_for` until that debt closes and listed *with <seat> since*; the outcome's line back on the look as `looked_by`, drawn above its answers; the refusals in the host agent's words; the techlead preset says how a handed look ends: one outcome whose text is its reading, or `blocked`.

**Done when:** a builder's leaned-Works look reaches Paul as a `steer` under *Steering* with its screenshots drawn and lapses to *Works* with no press, one nobody settled is an `ask` under *Needs you* with **Works** / **Not right…**, and **Send to reviewer** fills the techlead seat and brings the look back with the seat's reading.

**Related:** TD-290 (the design), TD-291 (the builder's half), TD-255 (a live look's pair), TD-218 (a handed entry), TD-127 (a message's shape).

## TD-297: The parts of the live looks of 2026-10-03 nobody could see

**Priority:** Low
**Type:** debt
**Added:** 2026-10-03 (the anchor, with TD-296)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Open — **Owner anchor from 2026-10-05 (grinder-ao-1): what is left is out of a scratch home's reach** — a check that waits on a logged-in session or on GitHub is made by whoever has them, and a live event when it happens. **A team stop time on members' cards does not hold** (pressed the same day with a shell member: the record and Focus take it, the Org draws it nowhere): TD-335. Every press still listed needs a logged-in claude-code session (a scratch home's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is its own, so none logs in: a role chip's `prompts:`, Resume, a permission card's Deny and keys, a team Start or `ao new me --team`, `stalled?`, a second agent session, Add entry's session, a live team's reserve line) or GitHub (`auto`'s green checks, the Org's *over its line*), and the rest are live events, read when they happen. **seven presses seen working 2026-10-04 (grinder-ao-1)** on a scratch home (`scripts/look_home.py`, its repo given a local origin so Ready to close passes), in headless Chromium: Close session is in the Focus header once Ready to close passes, drawn with no reload, and Close takes the record to `closed` (TD-156); Close and Kill each leave one *pane is gone* line after one *retrying in 1s*, and no websocket opens in the six seconds after (TD-029); on a killed session `#compose` is disabled with *this session's process has ended*, and **→ Send** sends nothing and says so (TD-047); Message… from a card's *more ⋯*, ✉ Message in Focus and Reply on an Inbox `ask` row each open the mail dialog (TD-052); `j` rings an FYI note and `x` presses its Dismiss, which confirms and removes it (TD-124); Put on the board opens a four-row box where Enter adds a line and Ctrl+Enter puts it on, the lines joined into one line at the top of *Needs the user*, one commit (TD-281); `ao new --unattended --at +2h` draws a *scheduled · starts <time>* card whose *more ⋯* holds Start now and Cancel, Cancel confirms and forgets the record, and `ao at <id> now` starts it on the next tick (TD-152). Those are struck from the Fix's list. **Three more the same day, on the same home:** a board row's answers are drawn in the order written with the default marked *DEFAULT*, the body without the tail, and **Go with it** in the foot after Reply; a click on an answer, **Go with it** (its confirm names the default), `1` and `4` on a ringed row and `g` each write the decision as one commit (`agentorc: decide <head>: <answer> (session look)`) and toast *decided: <answer> — committed on the board, not pushed*; **Not right…** on a look line opens the Reply composer with *Not right:* begun and writes the words as the decision, toasting *no entry was handed on: no team services repo: there is no techlead seat …* on a home with no techlead (TD-255); *due this week* on Settings → You saves `board_show: 7d` and the Inbox's line reads *showing board items due this week · 1 not shown, the next due Jan 1 — show* (TD-220); **Drop** on a claim with no PR is under the Reports card's *more ▾* in Focus, its confirm names the lease and the one session that may claim it again, and the session's inbox gains *your claim on TD-001 was dropped by the person — the lease is gone* (TD-150, TD-028). Struck too. **Snooze on an `unpushed` row** (an exited session whose repo is ahead of origin) offers *in 1 hour*, *tomorrow 08:00* and *a date and time…*, and the row moves under *1 snoozed — show* (TD-079); struck. The trail row the same home drew for an `unpushed` row that ended by a push read *resolved: resolved ×2*, where §4.10 says *pushed*: TD-308. **Three more 2026-10-05 (grinder-ao-1)**, on a scratch home whose repo had a local bare origin and a `promote:` block (`check` reading a file the `run` writes), in headless Chromium: a `run` that exits 1, pressed with `ao promote repo`, draws a counted *Needs you* row reading *failed: promoting cdc6c41 — the run ended and live is 04cd6aa, not cdc6c41 (exit 1). Nothing is promoted for repo until this is dismissed or a press succeeds.* with the log's tail; a second merge on origin is read (*main 4359875, 2 ahead*) and promotes nothing while the failure stands; **Dismiss** toasts *the failure is cleared: promoting goes on* and the row is a plain promote row again (TD-132 (3)); **Promote** on the row, with a `run` that works, toasts *promoting repo to f556e05 — checks read unknown, pressed through* and live reaches main; with `auto` on, `ao promote repo --back` makes the earlier commit live, and `ao promote status` and the Inbox row (under *Needs you*) read *live 04cd6aa, rolled back from f556e05 … · auto on · held*, with FYI notes *rolled back repo to … — main is n commits ahead*; the row's **Dismiss** toasts *the hold is ended: live stays where it is, and promoting goes on* and the hold is gone from `ao promote status` (TD-226); **Start** on a stopped team whose only member's lane matches nothing opens the dialog *Start lk-grind? · Nothing to pick: every member's lane is empty, so the team will wind down as soon as it starts. · grinder: 0 · Cancel · Start anyway*, and Cancel closes it with the team still stopped (TD-265, TD-262; *Start anyway* not pressed, since it launches claude-code sessions a scratch home cannot log in). Struck. Not pressable there: `auto` promoting by itself (TD-132 (2)) needs green checks, which a repo with no GitHub remote never reads (*checks unknown*); **Snooze on a `stalled?` row** needs a hook-fed session, since only those stall (`agent_tick`: a shell is `working` or `idle`). **Two more the same day, on a second scratch home:** on an unattended shell session with a team, Focus draws **Take over**; once pressed, *more ▾* holds **Hand back** (a session with no team and no controllers reads *Switch to unattended* there, as `cards.py` words it), and Hand back turns the record unattended again, the composer closed (TD-096); on a team the home's `org.yml` defines, with the home made a work tree, **Members…** opens *Members of lk-org*, *stopped: an edit is the definition only — the next Start brings it*, the name prefilled *grinder-lk-2*, and **Add member** lists it, toasts *org.yml: added grinder-lk-2 (grinder) — the team is stopped: its next Start brings the member*, writes one line into `org.yml`, and the home commits it as *org: lk-org added grinder-lk-2 (grinder)* (TD-268). Struck.
**Blocked by:** TD-335
**Location:** the closed look lines of 2026-10-03 in `docs/user_attention_archive.md` (each names its TD); design §4.5, §4.5a

**Why:** the looks were made read-only against the live copy, and presses only on isolated scratch homes, two of which stopped early (TD-298). What follows was not seen. None waits on Paul.

**Fix:** make each on a scratch home (TD-291's `scripts/look_home.py`) where a press settles it, or read-only the next time the live event happens; a failure is its own entry. **Presses:** a role chip sending `prompts:` (TD-170, needs a claude-code session); Resume on an exited card (TD-155); Deny's *why?* box (TD-117); `a`/`d` keys on a permission card; New session's Start and Wind down for a team (TD-173, TD-276) and `ao new me --team`; Snooze on a `stalled?` row (TD-079, needs a hook-fed session); Promote's `auto` (TD-132 (2), needs a repo whose checks read green); a profile reserve's chip line after Save, the Org's amber *over its line* (TD-244 Settings); Add entry → Hand to the techlead and → Open a session on the live repo page (TD-219, TD-283, with a logged-in session); a second agent session in a directory refused (TD-152; a shell is exempt, so it needs an agent session). **Live events:** an early or repeat restart mark reaching the person, with the row naming it (TD-249's last case); a member steering you with nothing else to pick (TD-274's wind-down part); *merged without its read* and either row's Dismiss (TD-258); the Inbox restart row, `ao restart`, an exited Focus banner (TD-250); a manager fill by cause (TD-259); a *reported* reading under a minute after a new session (TD-233 s2) and *· projected n%* (TD-233 s4); *closed by you / by manager-ao-1 / itself* and a wind-down within ten minutes (TD-265); an orphaned question (TD-216); the team-start row on the Inbox (TD-244 Settings); the origin row turning live with no reload after a pull, and agentorc's checkout catching up once the anchor is idle (TD-281, TD-263); *earlier turns* on a transcript of more than 20 turns (TD-166); four restarts in two hours and *early: nothing reported done* (TD-249); *brief: changed* in `ao status -v` (TD-217); a SendMessage to a worker refused (TD-064); a metered profile (TD-151, once one is declared).

**Carried from TD-255 at its archive (2026-10-03), a design gap, not a look:** a team whose manager is on call has no round, so a live look decided *Works* reaches no holder to close it, and the pair has no help entry. Whoever designs TD-290's rework (looks as mail) settles who closes a decided look; until then the anchor closes them.

**Done when:** each is seen working or ledgered as its own entry.

**Related:** TD-296, TD-291, TD-244.

## TD-299: Run the guardians and contractmatch teams inside their devcontainers

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature

The image's postCreate *copying credentials from stuff_for_containers_home* copies nothing on the node: that mount is a tmpfs there. **(3) and (4) done 2026-10-03:** the node's `env` (values unquoted: `docker --env-file` keeps quotes) and three profile logins; `place: {cm-grind: contractmatch}` and the project's node path in `org.yml`; the team started on the node, and grinder-cm-1 merged #110–#113 from inside (none touches `frontend/`: a frontend PR checked inside is still to come). Found on the way: TD-301 (hooks, built and live), TD-302 (a node session's own word is refused) and TD-316 (a node rebuild replaces its container and ends the sessions inside without a word: grinder-cm-1 and manager-cm-1 at 23:38). **Left:** contractmatch's brief still sends frontend work to the devcontainer (Paul's cm session is changing it); then guardians, (5). guardians works from the umbrella repo (Paul, 2026-10-03): its team is defined in `guardians-devenv` against that repo's ledger and board alone
**Location:** `~/.agentorc/hosts.yml` (`nodes:`), `~/.agentorc/nodes/<name>/` (`env`, `profiles/`), `~/.agentorc/org.yml` (`place:`), `/home/kmaster/guardians-devenv/.agentorc.yml` (new), `docs/briefs/guardians-orchestrator.md`, `/home/kmaster/contractmatch/.agentorc.yml`; design §4.4a *A container node*, §4.9 *Where a repo's team lands*, §10

**Why:** the hold of 2026-09-23 is lifted: guardians' five repos are checked out at `~/guardians-devenv`, §10 decided that a devcontainer running an `agentorc-agent` is a node, and the contractmatch node reads *running · link: up — linked* (`ao host status contractmatch`). A team in its container gets the project's own toolchain (contractmatch's `frontend/**` PRs are left open today because kmaster cannot run `flutter analyze`), and an unattended worker's reach is the container's mounts, with a repo-scoped token, never the person's credentials. Nothing has run a team on a node yet: TD-057's *Done when* is that run.

**Fix:** (1) **contractmatch first, on the node already up:** check that the image has what a worker runs (`claude`, the toolchain, Python 3.12+ on the agent's PATH); (2) `ao host rebuild contractmatch` at the live version if the node's build is behind; (3) **Paul's, once per node:** `~/.agentorc/nodes/<name>/env` (`0600`) with a fine-grained GitHub token scoped to the repo, the author name and email, and the repo's own needs (contractmatch: a Doppler service token), and one `claude` login inside per profile the node's roles name (`/agentorc/profiles/<profile>/`); (4) `place: {cm-grind: contractmatch}` in `org.yml` with the team wound down, then Start, and watch one entry go from pick to merged PR from inside, a frontend one included; (5) **guardians:** a `nodes: guardians: container: {devcontainer: ~/guardians-devenv}` entry and `ao host up guardians`, step (3) for it, then `guardians-devenv/.agentorc.yml` with a team in the manager / techlead / members shape the other repos use (by a PR in that repo), `place:` to its node, and `docs/briefs/guardians-orchestrator.md` rewritten from the old lead-and-grant shape (a held path: the techlead's read). The five-repo constellation (cadence §9): the team works from the umbrella's ledger and board alone (Paul, 2026-10-03).

**Done when:** cm-grind and a guardians team each run on their node, each with a PR merged from inside, and TD-057's *Done when* reads met.

**Related:** TD-057 (the home and node split), TD-229 (repo-defined teams and `place:`), TD-296/TD-298 (2026-10-03's looks).
